To derive the bound, for an arbitrary layer $q' \in \{q+1, \dots, Q\}$, we begin by expressing $\mathbf{K}_{t, j}^{(q')}$ in terms of the layer's hidden-state sensitivity. Then, by definition:
\begin{equation}
    \left(\mathbf{K}_{t,j}^{(q')}\right)_{d,d'} = \frac{\partial y_{t,d}^{(q')}}{\partial x_{j,d'}^{(q')}} = \sum_{n=1}^N C_{t,n}^{(q')} \frac{\partial h_{t,n,d}^{(q')}}{\partial x_{j,d'}^{(q')}} + \mathbbm{1}_{j=t} \sum_{n=1}^N h_{t,n,d}^{(q')}\left(W_C^{(q')}\right)_{n,d'}, \label{eq:multi_layer_K_matrix_coordinate_expression}
\end{equation}
for $1 \leq j \leq t \leq S$ and $d, d' \in \{1, \dots, D\}$.

Next, by definition of the spectral norm, we know that:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:multi_layer_K_matrix_spectral_def}
    \left\|\mathbf{K}_{t,j}^{(q')}\right\|_2 = \sup_{\substack{u \in \mathbb R^D \\ \|u\|_2 = 1}} \left\|\mathbf{K}_{t,j}^{(q')}u\right\|_2.
\end{equation}

As such, we first evaluate the value of $\mathbf{K}_{t,j}^{(q')}u$ for some arbitrary unit vector $u \in \mathbb R^D$. Multiplying~\eqref{eq:multi_layer_K_matrix_coordinate_expression} by $u_{d'}$, summing over $d'$, and collecting the output coordinates, we get:
\begin{equation} 
    \mathbf{K}_{t,j}^{(q')}u = \left(\sum_{d'=1}^D u_{d'}\frac{\partial h_t^{(q')}}{\partial x_{j,d'}^{(q')}}\right)^\top C_t^{(q')} + \mathbbm{1}_{j=t} \left(h_t^{(q')}\right)^\top W_C^{(q')}u.
\end{equation}

Note that, since $u$ is a unit vector, the sum in the first term is, by definition, the directional derivative of the hidden state with respect to $x_{j}^{(q')}$ in direction $u$. We denote it as:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:multi_layer_directional_derivative_hidden_state}
    D_{x_j^{(q')}} h_t^{(q')}[u] := \sum_{d'=1}^D u_{d'}\frac{\partial h_t^{(q')}}{\partial x_{j,d'}^{(q')}} \in \mathbb R^{N \times D}.
\end{equation}
As such, it follows that:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:multi_layer_K_matrix_directional_derivative_expression}
    \mathbf{K}_{t,j}^{(q')}u = \left(D_{x_j^{(q')}} h_t^{(q')}[u]\right)^\top C_t^{(q')} + \mathbbm{1}_{j=t} \left(h_t^{(q')}\right)^\top W_C^{(q')}u
\end{equation}

We then bound both terms of the sum in~\eqref{eq:multi_layer_K_matrix_directional_derivative_expression} separately. 

We evaluate the term $\left(D_{x_j^{(q')}} h_t^{(q')}[u]\right)^\top C_t^{(q')}$ first. Recall that by~\eqref{eq:hidden_state_definition_multi_layer}:
\begin{equation*}
    h_t^{(q')} = \bar{A}_t^{(q')} \odot h_{t-1}^{(q')} + \bar{B}_t^{(q')} \operatorname{diag}\left(x_t^{(q')}\right).
\end{equation*}
Since, for fixed layer $q'$ weights, $\bar{A}_t^{(q')}$ and $\bar{B}_t^{(q')}$ depend only on the input $x_t^{(q')}$, their derivatives with respect to $x_j^{(q')}$ vanish when $t\neq j$. Furthermore, $D_{x_j^{(q')}} x_t^{(q')}[u] = \mathbbm{1}_{j=t}u$. As such, applying these results and the product rule, we get:
\begin{align}
    D_{x_j^{(q')}} h_t^{(q')}[u] = \bar{A}_t^{(q')} \odot D_{x_j^{(q')}} h_{t-1}^{(q')}[u] &+ \mathbbm{1}_{j=t}\bigg[\left(D_{x_t^{(q')}} \bar{A}_t^{(q')}[u]\right)\odot h_{t-1}^{(q')} + \nonumber \\ & \left(D_{x_t^{(q')}} \bar{B}_t^{(q')}[u]\right)\operatorname{diag}\left(x_t^{(q')}\right) + \bar{B}_t^{(q')} \operatorname{diag}(u) \bigg],
\end{align}
where we also used the fact that $D_{x_j^{(q')}} \operatorname{diag}\left(x_t^{(q')}\right)[u] = \mathbbm{1}_{j=t}\operatorname{diag}\left(u\right)$. 

Next, we consider three cases. When $0 \leq t < j$, because the hidden state is initialized to $0$ and its evolution is causal, then:
\begin{equation}
    D_{x_j^{(q')}} h_t^{(q')}[u] = \mathbf{0}, \qquad \forall t \in \{0, \dots, j-1\}.
\end{equation}

When, $t=j$, $D_{x_j^{(q')}} h_{t-1}^{(q')}[u] = D_{x_t^{(q')}} h_{t-1}^{(q')}[u] = \mathbf{0}$, so:
\begin{equation}
    D_{x_t^{(q')}} h_{t}^{(q')}[u] = \left(D_{x_t^{(q')}} \bar{A}_t^{(q')}[u]\right)\odot h_{t-1}^{(q')} + \left(D_{x_t^{(q')}} \bar{B}_t^{(q')}[u]\right)\operatorname{diag}\left(x_t^{(q')}\right) + \bar{B}_t^{(q')} \operatorname{diag}(u).
\end{equation}

Finally, for $t > j$, the recurrence becomes:
\begin{equation}
    D_{x_j^{(q')}} h_t^{(q')}[u] = \bar{A}_t^{(q')} \odot D_{x_j^{(q')}} h_{t-1}^{(q')}[u].
\end{equation}

Next, by equation~\eqref{eq:rho_q_relationship_A_max}, which follows from definition~\ref{def:multilayer_ssm_stability}, we obtain:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:recursive_definition_of_D_h_d_x}
    \left\|D_{x_j^{(q')}} h_t^{(q')}[u]\right\|_F \leq \rho_{q'}\left\|D_{x_j^{(q')}} h_{t-1}^{(q')}[u]\right\|_F \leq \rho_{q'}^{t-j} \left\|D_{x_j^{(q')}} h_j^{(q')}[u]\right\|_F, \qquad \forall t > j.
\end{equation}

As a result, we just need to bound $\left\|D_{x_j^{(q')}} h_{t}^{(q')}[u] \right\|_F$ for the case when $t=j$, and  apply the recursive relationship. By the triangle inequality, when $t=j$, it follows that:
\begin{align}
    \left\|D_{x_j^{(q')}} h_{j}^{(q')}[u] \right\|_F \leq &\left\|\left(D_{x_j^{(q')}} \bar{A}_j^{(q')}[u]\right)\odot h_{j-1}^{(q')}\right\|_F \nonumber \\ &+ \left\|\left(D_{x_j^{(q')}} \bar{B}_j^{(q')}[u]\right)\operatorname{diag}\left(x_j^{(q')}\right)\right\|_F + \left\|\bar{B}_j^{(q')} \operatorname{diag}(u)\right\|_F
\end{align}

Then, evaluating it term by term
\begin{align}
    D_{x_j^{(q')}} \bar{A}_j^{(q')}[u] &= D_{x_j^{(q')}} \left(\exp\left(A^{(q')}\left(\Delta_j^{(q')}\right)^\top\right)\right)[u] \nonumber \\ &= \exp\left(A^{(q')}\left(\Delta_j^{(q')}\right)^\top\right) \odot \left[A^{(q')}\left(D_{x_j^{(q')}}\Delta_j^{(q')}[u]\right)^\top\right] \nonumber \\ &= \bar{A}_j^{(q')} \odot \left[A^{(q')}\left(D_{x_j^{(q')}}\Delta_j^{(q')}[u]\right)^\top\right].
\end{align}

Using the definition:
\begin{align}
    D_{x_j^{(q')}}\Delta_j^{(q')}[u] = D_{x_j^{(q')}}\left(\operatorname{Softplus}\left(W_\Delta^{(q')}x_j^{(q')}\right) \right)[u] = \operatorname{Softplus}'\left(W_\Delta^{(q')}x_j^{(q')}\right) \odot \left(W_\Delta^{(q')}u\right).
\end{align}

Next, since $\operatorname{Softplus}'(z) = \frac{1}{1+e^{-z}}\in(0,1)$, we get:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:d_delta_dx_bound}
    \left\|D_{x_j^{(q')}}\Delta_j^{(q')}[u]\right\|_2 \leq \left\|W_\Delta^{(q')}\right\|_2 \|u\|_2 = \left\|W_\Delta^{(q')}\right\|_2 .
\end{equation}
Then, the fact that $\|ab^\top\|_F = \|a\|_2\|b\|_2$ (which follows from the definition of the Frobenius norm), the following holds: 
\begin{align}
    \left\|D_{x_j^{(q')}} \bar{A}_j^{(q')}[u] \right\|_F &\leq  \left\|\bar{A}_j^{(q')}\right\|_{\max} \left\| A^{(q')}\left(D_{x_j^{(q')}}\Delta_j^{(q')}[u]\right)^\top\right\|_F \nonumber \\ &=\left\|\bar{A}_j^{(q')}\right\|_{\max} \left\| A^{(q')}\right\|_2\left\|D_{x_j^{(q')}}\Delta_j^{(q')}[u]\right\|_2 \leq \rho_{q'} \left\| A^{(q')}\right\|_2 \left\|W_\Delta^{(q')}\right\|_2,\label{eq:d_A_x_j_result}
\end{align}
Finally, combining equations~\eqref{eq:d_A_x_j_result} and~\eqref{eq:multi_layer_state_bound} yields:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:d_A_x_h_j_result}
    \left\|\left(D_{x_j^{(q')}} \bar{A}_j^{(q')}[u]\right)\odot h_{j-1}^{(q')}\right\|_F \leq \left\|D_{x_j^{(q')}} \bar{A}_j^{(q')}[u]\right\|_F\left\| h_{j-1}^{(q')}\right\|_F \leq \rho_{q'} \left\| A^{(q')}\right\|_2 \left\|W_\Delta^{(q')}\right\|_2 H_{{q'}}.
\end{equation}

For the second term, from the definition of $\bar{B}_{j}^{(q')}$, and using the product rule, we have:
\begin{equation}
    D_{x_j^{(q')}} \bar{B}_j^{(q')}[u] = \left(W_B^{(q')}u\right)\left(\Delta_j^{(q')}\right)^\top + \left(W_B^{(q')}x_j^{(q')}\right) \left(D_{x_j^{(q')}}\Delta_j^{(q')}[u]\right)^\top
\end{equation}
Then, combining the result of~\eqref{eq:d_delta_dx_bound} with 
\begin{equation}
    \left\|\Delta_{j}^{(q')}\right\|_2 \leq  \left\|W_\Delta^{(q')}x_{j}^{(q')}\right\|_2 + \sqrt{D}\log(2) \leq K_{\Delta,q'},
\end{equation}
we get:
\begin{align}
    \left\|D_{x_j^{(q')}} \bar{B}_j^{(q')}[u]\right\|_F & \leq \left\|W_B^{(q')}u\right\|_2\left\|\Delta_j^{(q')}\right\|_2 + \left\|W_B^{(q')}x_j^{(q')}\right\|_2 \left\|D_{x_j^{(q')}}\Delta_j^{(q')}[u]\right\|_2 \nonumber \\ &\leq \left\|W_B^{(q')}\right\|_2 K_{\Delta,q'} + \left\|W_B^{(q')}\right\|_2 L_{x,q'}\left\|W_\Delta^{(q')}\right\|_2.
\end{align}

Finally, since $\left\|\operatorname{diag}\left(v\right)\right\|_2 = \max_{d}\left|v_d\right|= \left\|v\right\|_{\infty} \leq \left\|v\right\|_2$ (and therefore $\left\|\operatorname{diag}\left(x_j^{(q')}\right)\right\|_2 \leq L_{x,q'}$), the second term is bounded as:
\begin{align}
    \left\|\left(D_{x_j^{(q')}} \bar{B}_j^{(q')}[u]\right)\operatorname{diag}\left(x_j^{(q')}\right)\right\|_F  &\leq \left\|D_{x_j^{(q')}} \bar{B}_j^{(q')}[u]\right\|_F\left\|\operatorname{diag}\left(x_j^{(q')}\right)\right\|_2 \nonumber \\ & \leq \left\|W_B^{(q')}\right\|_2  L_{x,q'} \left(K_{\Delta,q'} + L_{x,q'}\left\|W_\Delta^{(q')}\right\|_2\right) \label{eq:d_B_diag_x_result}
\end{align}

For the remaining term, the bound is as follows:
\begin{align}
    \left\|\bar{B}_j^{(q')} \operatorname{diag}(u)\right\|_F &\leq \left\|\bar{B}_j^{(q')}\right\|_F \left\| \operatorname{diag}(u)\right\|_2 \nonumber \\ &\leq \left\|W_B^{(q')}x_j^{(q')}\right\|_2 \left\|\Delta_j^{(q')}\right\|_2 \left\| u\right\|_2 \nonumber \\ &\leq \left\|W_B^{(q')}\right\|_2 L_{x,q'}K_{\Delta,q'} \label{eq:d_b_u__result}
\end{align}

Combining the three bounds, we get:
\begin{align}
    \left\|D_{x_j^{(q')}} h_{j}^{(q')}[u] \right\|_F \leq \rho_{q'} \left\| A^{(q')}\right\|_2 \left\|W_\Delta^{(q')}\right\|_2 H_{{q'}} + \left\|W_B^{(q')}\right\|_2  L_{x,q'} \left(2K_{\Delta,q'} + L_{x,q'}\left\|W_\Delta^{(q')}\right\|_2\right).
\end{align}

For ease of notation, for each layer $q'$, we define:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:Lambda_x_q_prime}
    \Lambda_{x,q'} := \rho_{q'} \left\| A^{(q')}\right\|_2 \left\|W_\Delta^{(q')}\right\|_2 H_{{q'}} + \left\|W_B^{(q')}\right\|_2  L_{x,q'} \left(2K_{\Delta,q'} + L_{x,q'}\left\|W_\Delta^{(q')}\right\|_2\right).
\end{equation}

Using this notation, we have the following bound:
\begin{equation}
    \left\|D_{x_j^{(q')}} h_{j}^{(q')}[u] \right\|_F \leq \Lambda_{x,q'}, \qquad \|u\|_2 = 1.
\end{equation}

Substituting this bound into the recurrence established in~\eqref{eq:recursive_definition_of_D_h_d_x}, we get:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:final_D_ht_dxj_u}
    \left\|D_{x_j^{(q')}} h_t^{(q')}[u]\right\|_F \leq \rho_{q'}^{t-j} \left\|D_{x_j^{(q')}} h_j^{(q')}[u]\right\|_F \leq \Lambda_{x,q'}\rho_{q'}^{t-j}, \qquad 1 \leq j \leq t \leq S.
\end{equation}
given $\|u\|_2 = 1$, and $\left\|D_{x_j^{(q')}} h_t^{(q')}[u]\right\|_F=0$ for $t < j$.

Returning to the expression for $\mathbf{K}_{t,j}^{(q')}u$ in~\eqref{eq:multi_layer_K_matrix_directional_derivative_expression}, we can bound the first term of the sum using the bound derived in~\eqref{eq:final_D_ht_dxj_u} (and the fact that $\left\|Z^\top c\right\|_2 \leq \|Z\|_F\|c\|_2$) as follows:
\begin{equation}
    \left\|\left(D_{x_j^{(q')}} h_t^{(q')}[u]\right)^\top C_t^{(q')}\right\|_2 \leq \left\|D_{x_j^{(q')}} h_t^{(q')}[u]\right\|_F\left\|C_t^{(q')}\right\|_2 \leq \left\|W_C^{(q')}\right\|_2 L_{x,q'}\Lambda_{x,q'}\rho_{q'}^{t-j}.
\end{equation}

For the second term, we can bound this as:
\begin{equation}
    \left\|\mathbbm{1}_{j=t}\left(h_t^{(q')}\right)^\top W_C^{(q')}u\right\|_2 \leq \mathbbm{1}_{j=t} \left\|h_t^{(q')}\right\|_F\left\|W_C^{(q')}\right\|_2\left\|u\right\|_2 = \mathbbm{1}_{j=t} \left\|h_t^{(q')}\right\|_F\left\|W_C^{(q')}\right\|_2
\end{equation}

Using the triangle inequality, and combining the derived bounds on the terms of the sum in~\eqref{eq:multi_layer_K_matrix_directional_derivative_expression}, we get:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:final_ku_2_norm_bound}
    \left\|\mathbf{K}_{t,j}^{(q')}u\right\|_2 \leq \left\|W_C^{(q')}\right\|_2L_{x,q'}\Lambda_{x,q'}\rho_{q'}^{t-j} + \mathbbm{1}_{j=t} \left\|W_C^{(q')}\right\|_2H_{{q'}}.
\end{equation}

Since the right hand side of~\eqref{eq:final_ku_2_norm_bound} is independent of $u$, and therefore holds uniformly for all unit vectors $u$, taking the supremum over all $u$, we get:
\begin{equation}
    \left\|\mathbf{K}_{t,j}^{(q')}\right\|_2 \leq \left\|W_C^{(q')}\right\|_2L_{x,q'}\Lambda_{x,q'}\rho_{q'}^{t-j} + \mathbbm{1}_{j=t} \left\|W_C^{(q')}\right\|_2H_{{q'}}, \qquad 1 \leq j \leq t \leq S.
\end{equation}


Next, for every fixed position $t$, summing $\left\|\mathbf{K}_{t,j}^{(q')}\right\|_2 $ over $j$ yields:
\begin{align}
    \sum_{j=1}^t\left\|\mathbf{K}_{t,j}^{(q')}\right\|_2  &\leq \sum_{j=1}^t\left(\left\|W_C^{(q')}\right\|_2L_{x,q'}\Lambda_{x,q'}\rho_{q'}^{t-j} + \mathbbm{1}_{j=t} \left\|W_C^{(q')}\right\|_2H_{{q'}}\right) \nonumber \\ &= \left\|W_C^{(q')}\right\|_2L_{x,q'}\Lambda_{x,q'}\sum_{j=1}^t\rho_{q'}^{t-j} + \left\|W_C^{(q')}\right\|_2H_{{q'}} \nonumber \\ 
    &= \left\|W_C^{(q')}\right\|_2L_{x,q'}\Lambda_{x,q'}\frac{1-\rho_{q'}^t}{1-\rho_{q'}} + \left\|W_C^{(q')}\right\|_2H_{{q'}} \nonumber \\ &\leq \left\|W_C^{(q')}\right\|_2\left(H_{{q'}}+ \frac{L_{x,q'}\Lambda_{x,q'}}{1-\rho_{q'}}\right) = \kappa_{q'},
\end{align}
where, for each layer $q'$, we define the uniform layerwise input-output sensitivity bound 
\begin{equation}\label{eq:kappa_definition}
    \kappa_{q'}:=\left\|W_C^{(q')}\right\|_2\left(H_{{q'}}+ \frac{L_{x,q'}\Lambda_{x,q'}}{1-\rho_{q'}}\right) 
\end{equation}

Next, since $\kappa_{q'}$ is independent of $t$, it follows that:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:multi_layer_K_temporal_sum_bound_uniform}
    \max_{1 \leq t \leq S}\sum_{j=1}^t\left\|\mathbf{K}_{t,j}^{(q')}\right\|_2 \leq \kappa_{q'}.
\end{equation}
Since $W$ satisfies the stated stability and bounded-input conditions, the bound holds uniformly over all admissible input-target sequences and token indices. 

Now, returning to~\eqref{eq:backprop_term_additon}, consider the two inner-most sums (for $q \leq Q-2$):
\begin{align}
    \sum_{j_{q+1}=1}^{j_{q+2}}\sum_{j_{q}=1}^{j_{q+1}}\left[\prod_{q'=q+1}^Q \left\|\mathbf{K}_{j_{q'},j_{q'-1}}^{(q')}\right\|_2\right] &= \sum_{j_{q+1}=1}^{j_{q+2}}\sum_{j_{q}=1}^{j_{q+1}}\left\|\mathbf{K}_{j_{q+1},j_{q}}^{(q+1)}\right\|_2\left\|\mathbf{K}_{j_{q+2},j_{q+1}}^{(q+2)}\right\|_2\cdots\left\|\mathbf{K}_{j_{Q},j_{Q-1}}^{(Q)}\right\|_2 \nonumber \\ &= \sum_{j_{q+1}=1}^{j_{q+2}}\left[\sum_{j_{q}=1}^{j_{q+1}}\left\|\mathbf{K}_{j_{q+1},j_{q}}^{(q+1)}\right\|_2\left\|\mathbf{K}_{j_{q+2},j_{q+1}}^{(q+2)}\right\|_2\cdots\left\|\mathbf{K}_{j_{Q},j_{Q-1}}^{(Q)}\right\|_2\right] \nonumber \\ &= \sum_{j_{q+1}=1}^{j_{q+2}}\left\|\mathbf{K}_{j_{q+2},j_{q+1}}^{(q+2)}\right\|_2\cdots\left\|\mathbf{K}_{j_{Q},j_{Q-1}}^{(Q)}\right\|_2\left[\sum_{j_{q}=1}^{j_{q+1}}\left\|\mathbf{K}_{j_{q+1},j_{q}}^{(q+1)}\right\|_2\right] \nonumber \\ &\leq  \sum_{j_{q+1}=1}^{j_{q+2}}\left\|\mathbf{K}_{j_{q+2},j_{q+1}}^{(q+2)}\right\|_2\cdots\left\|\mathbf{K}_{j_{Q},j_{Q-1}}^{(Q)}\right\|_2\kappa_{q+1} \nonumber \\ & = \kappa_{q+1}\sum_{j_{q+1}=1}^{j_{q+2}}\left\|\mathbf{K}_{j_{q+2},j_{q+1}}^{(q+2)}\right\|_2\cdots\left\|\mathbf{K}_{j_{Q},j_{Q-1}}^{(Q)}\right\|_2,
\end{align}
where the inequality follows from~\eqref{eq:multi_layer_K_temporal_sum_bound_uniform}. Since $\kappa_{q+1}$ is independent of all remaining sums, and we can apply the same argument successively to each remaining innermost sum, it follows that:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:nested_sum_bound_backprop_contribution}
    \sum_{j_{Q-1}=1}^{j_Q}\cdots\sum_{j_q=1}^{j_{q+1}}\left[ \prod_{q'=q+1}^{Q} \left\|\mathbf{K}_{j_{q'}, j_{q'-1}}^{(q')}\right\|_2\right]  \leq \kappa_{q+1} \kappa_{q+2} \cdots\kappa_{Q} = \prod_{q'=q+1}^Q \kappa_{q'}.
\end{equation}
for the fixed $W$ and every admissible input-target sequence pair. For $q=Q-1$, there is only one sum, and holds directly from definition: $\sum_{j_{Q-1}=1}^t \left\|\mathbf{K}_{t,j_{Q-1}}^{(Q)}\right\|_2 \leq \kappa_Q$.

Finally, combining the nested-sum bound in~\eqref{eq:nested_sum_bound_backprop_contribution} with~\eqref{eq:output_jacobian_norm_bound} and~\eqref{eq:multi_layer_own_output_jacobian_bound}, we obtain:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:multi_layer_final_output_jacobian_bound}
    \left\|\frac{\partial y_t^{(Q)}}{\partial W^{(q)}}\right\|_F \leq L_{x,q}\left(H_{q}+\left\|W_C^{(q)}\right\|_2J_{q}\right)\prod_{q'=q+1}^Q \kappa_{q'}.
\end{equation}

This bound holds for every $q \in \{1, \dots, Q\}$ and $t \in \{1, \dots, S\}$, where an empty product is interpreted as one. For the $q=Q$ case, the inequality follows directly from the own-layer bound in~\eqref{eq:multi_layer_own_output_jacobian_bound}. 

We now return to the loss gradient in~\eqref{eq:multi_layer_loss_gradient_expression}. Under Assumption~\ref{assum:lipschitz_cont_loss}, applied at the fixed full parameter tuple $W$ to final-layer predictions uniformly over admissible pairs and token indices, we have:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:nabla_ell_y_bound}
    \left\|\nabla_y \ell_t \left(y_t^{(Q)}\right)\right\|_2 \leq L_\ell.
\end{equation}

Finally, taking the Euclidean norm of the vectorized gradient in~\eqref{eq:multi_layer_loss_gradient_expression}, which equals its block Frobenius norm, gives:
\begin{align}
    \left\|\nabla_{W^{(q)}}\mathcal L^{S,(Q)}(W)\right\|_F &= \left\|\frac{1}{S}\sum_{t=1}^S\left(\frac{\partial y_t^{(Q)}}{\partial W^{(q)}}\right)^\top \nabla_y\ell_t\left(y_t^{(Q)}\right)\right\|_2 \nonumber \\ &\leq \frac{1}{S}\sum_{t=1}^S \left\|\nabla_y \ell_t \left(y_t^{(Q)}\right)\right\|_2 \left\|\frac{\partial y_t^{(Q)}}{\partial W^{(q)}}\right\|_F \nonumber \\ &\leq  \frac{1}{S}\sum_{t=1}^S L_{\ell} L_{x,q}\left(H_{q}+\left\|W_C^{(q)}\right\|_2J_{q}\right)\prod_{q'=q+1}^Q \kappa_{q'} \nonumber \\ &= L_{\ell} L_{x,q}\left(H_{q}+\left\|W_C^{(q)}\right\|_2J_{q}\right)\prod_{q'=q+1}^Q \kappa_{q'} \label{eq:multi_layer_loss_gradient_block_bound},
\end{align}
where we use~\eqref{eq:nabla_ell_y_bound}, ~\eqref{eq:multi_layer_final_output_jacobian_bound}, the triangle inequality, and the fact that $\|M^\top v\|_2\leq\|M\|_F\|v\|_2$. The bound is independent of the token index $t$.

Substituting the result of~\eqref{eq:multi_layer_loss_gradient_block_bound} back into~\eqref{eq:desired_bound_on_seq_S}, we get:
\begin{equation}
    \left\|\nabla_{W}\mathcal L^{S,(Q)}(W)\right\|_F \leq L_\ell \left  (\sum_{q=1}^Q L_{x,q}^2\left(H_{q} + \left\|W_C^{(q)}\right\|_2J_{q}\right)^2 \prod _{q'=q+1}^Q \kappa_{q'}^2\right)^{1/2} = \mathcal G_{\text{SSM}}^{(Q)}(W).
\end{equation}

Since $S\geq1$ and the admissible length-$S$ pair $\xi\in\mathcal Z$ were arbitrary, and the right-hand side is independent of $S$ and $\xi$, the bound holds uniformly over all such lengths and admissible pairs for the fixed full parameter tuple $W$, proving Theorem~\ref{thm:ssm_multi_layer_grad_bound}. \qed
<!-- end head-8 -->
<!-- lemma-6: body_md -->
\label{lem:multilayer_local_smoothness}
Under the neighborhood assumptions and parameter envelopes introduced above, there exists a finite constant $L_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf R_Q}^{(Q)}$ defined:
\begin{equation}
    L_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf R_Q}^{(Q)} := H_\ell\Theta_Q^2+L_\ell\Omega_Q,
\end{equation}
with $\Theta_Q$ and $\Omega_Q$ defined in~\eqref{eq:Theta_Q_def_lemma} and~\eqref{eq:lem_multilayer_second_sensitivity_recursion} respectively, such that, for every admissible sequence pair $\xi$ of finite length $S$ and every $W,V\in\mathcal N_{\mathbf R_Q}$,
\begin{equation}
    \left\|\nabla_W\mathcal L^{S,(Q)}(W;\xi) - \nabla_W\mathcal L^{S,(Q)}(V;\xi)\right\|_F \leq L_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf R_Q}^{(Q)}\|W-V\|_F,
\end{equation}
independent of $S$ and $\xi$.

Furthermore, if each client objective $\mathcal L_k$ is finite and differentiable on $\mathcal N_{\mathbf R_Q}$ and satisfies:
\begin{equation}
    \nabla_W\mathcal L_k(W) = \mathbb E_{\xi\sim\mathcal P_k}\left[\nabla_W\mathcal L^{S,(Q)}(W;\xi)\right],
\end{equation}
then every $\mathcal L_k$, and therefore $f=\sum_k p_k\mathcal L_k$, is also $L_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf R_Q}^{(Q)}$-smooth on $\mathcal N_{\mathbf R_Q}$.

$\Theta_Q$ and $\Omega_Q$ have the following recursive definitions:
\begin{align}
    \Theta_0 &:= 0, \qquad \Theta_q : =\left(L_{x,q}^2\left(H_{R_q}+M_{C,q}J_{R_q}\right)^2+M_{\kappa,q}^2\Theta_{q-1}^2\right)^{1/2} \label{eq:Theta_Q_def_lemma}, \\
    \Omega_0 &:= 0, \qquad 
    \Omega_q := \frac{M_{C,q}L_{x,q}\mathcal E_q}{1 - \rho_q} + 2\left(L_{x,q}+M_{C,q}\Theta_{q-1}\right)\left(J_{R_q} + \frac{M_{\Lambda_x,q}\Theta_{q-1}}{1-\rho_q}\right) \nonumber \\ &~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~\qquad + 2H_{R_q}\Theta_{q-1} + M_{\kappa,q}\Omega_{q-1}. \label{eq:lem_multilayer_second_sensitivity_recursion}
\end{align}
where:
\begin{align}
    \mathcal E_q :=& \rho_q H_{R_q} \Bigg[\left(M_{K_\Delta,q} + M_{A,q}\left(L_{x,q} + M_{\Delta,q}\Theta_{q-1}\right)\right)^2 + 2\left(L_{x,q} + M_{\Delta,q}\Theta_{q-1}\right) \nonumber \\
    &+ M_{A,q}\left(\frac{1}{4}\left(L_{x,q} + M_{\Delta,q}\Theta_{q-1}\right)^2 + 2\Theta_{q-1}\right)\Bigg] \nonumber \\
    &+ 2\rho_q\left(M_{K_\Delta,q} + M_{A,q}\left(L_{x,q} + M_{\Delta,q}\Theta_{q-1}\right)\right)\left(J_{R_q} + \frac{M_{\Lambda_x,q}\Theta_{q-1}}{1-\rho_q}\right)\nonumber \\
    & + 2\Theta_{q-1}M_{K_\Delta,q}L_{x,q}\nonumber \\
    & + 2\left(L_{x,q} + M_{B,q}\Theta_{q-1}\right)\left[L_{x,q}\left(L_{x,q} + M_{\Delta,q}\Theta_{q-1}\right) + M_{K_\Delta,q}\Theta_{q-1}\right] \nonumber \\
    & + M_{B,q}L_{x,q}\Bigg[L_{x,q}\left(\frac{1}{4}\left(L_{x,q} + M_{\Delta,q}\Theta_{q-1}\right)^2 + 2\Theta_{q-1}\right) + 2\left(L_{x,q} + M_{\Delta,q}\Theta_{q-1}\right)\Theta_{q-1}\Bigg].\label{eq:lem_multilayer_second_state_forcing}
\end{align}
<!-- end lemma-6 -->
<!-- head-9: body_md -->
Let $W \in \mathcal N_{\mathbf R_Q}$ be some fixed arbitrary parameter tuple, and $\xi=(x,y^*)$ be some admissible fixed input-target sequence pair of length $S \geq 1$. Note that, while $x$ and $y^*$ remain fixed throughout the differentiation, the intermediate inputs and outputs $x_t^{(q)}=y_t^{(q-1)}$ depend on the parameters of the preceding layers for $q \geq 2$. 

We introduce:
\begin{equation}
    U = \left(U^{(1)}, \dots, U^{(Q)}\right), \qquad V = \left(V^{(1)}, \dots, V^{(Q)}\right)
\end{equation}
as two arbitrary full-model perturbations such that:
\begin{equation}
    U^{(q)} = \left(U_\Delta^{(q)}, U_B^{(q)}, U_C^{(q)}, U_A^{(q)}\right), \qquad V^{(q)} = \left(V_\Delta^{(q)}, V_B^{(q)}, V_C^{(q)}, V_A^{(q)}\right)
\end{equation}
and:
\begin{equation}
    \|U\|_F^2 := \sum_{q=1}^Q\left\|U^{(q)}\right\|_F^2 = 1, \qquad \|V\|_F^2 := \sum_{q=1}^Q\left\|V^{(q)}\right\|_F^2 = 1.
\end{equation}
Note that each layerwise norm such as $\left\|U^{(q)}\right\|_F$ is defined as the Euclidean combination of the Frobenius norms of its four parameter blocks. As a result, every layerwise direction and every individual parameter-block direction has norm at most one. 

Then, for a twice continuously differentiable function $F$ of the full parameter tuple, we define the first and second directional derivatives by
\begin{align}
    DF(W)[U] &:= \frac{d}{d\epsilon} F(W+\epsilon U) \bigg|_{\epsilon = 0}, \\
    D^2F(W)[U,V] &:= \frac{\partial^2}{\partial\epsilon\partial\zeta}F(W + \epsilon U+\zeta V)\bigg|_{\epsilon = \zeta = 0}.
\end{align}
Unless otherwise specified, all derivatives in the remaining part of this proof are taken with respect to the full parameter tuple $W$.

The first-order chain rule gives
\begin{equation}
    D\left(\ell_t\left(y_t^{(Q)}\right)\right)(W)[U] = \left\langle \nabla_y\ell_t\left(y_t^{(Q)}(W)\right), Dy_t^{(Q)}(W)[U] \right\rangle.
\end{equation}
where we used the notation $\ell_t(y) = \ell_t(y,y_t^*)$. By differentiating this expression in $V$ direction, we get:
\begin{align}
    D^2\left(\ell_t\left(y_t^{(Q)}\right)\right)(W)[U,V] =& \left(Dy_t^{(Q)}(W)[U]\right)^\top\nabla_y^2\ell_t\left(y_t^{(Q)}(W)\right)Dy_t^{(Q)}(W)[V] \nonumber \\ 
    &+ \left\langle\nabla_y\ell_t\left(y_t^{(Q)}(W)\right),D^2y_t^{(Q)}(W)[U,V]\right\rangle.
    \label{eq:multilayer_token_loss_second_derivative}
\end{align}

By the neighborhood-uniform loss-regularity bounds introduced in Assumption~\ref{assum:lipschitz_cont_loss} and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, it follows that
\begin{align}
    \Bigg|D^2\left(\ell_t\left(y_t^{(Q)}\right)\right)(W)[U,V]\Bigg| \leq & H_\ell \left\|Dy_t^{(Q)}(W)[U]\right\|_2 \left\|Dy_t^{(Q)}(W)[V]\right\|_2 + L_\ell \left\|D^2y_t^{(Q)}(W)[U,V]\right\|_2. \label{eq:multilayer_token_loss_hessian_reduction}
\end{align}

As such, to bound the second derivative of the loss, it suffices to derive uniform bounds on the first and second directional derivatives of the final-layer output. 

We first establish the first-order output sensitivity bounds needed for the second-order calculation.

For ease of notation, for a full-model direction $U$ we write $U^{(\leq q)}=\left(U^{(1)},\dots,U^{(q)}\right)$ and $U^{(<q)}=(U^{(1)},\dots,U^{(q-1)})$. We further define the following recursion:
\begin{equation}
    \Theta_0 := 0, \qquad \Theta_q : =\left(L_{x,q}^2\left(H_{R_q}+M_{C,q}J_{R_q}\right)^2+M_{\kappa,q}^2\Theta_{q-1}^2\right)^{1/2}
\end{equation}

First, by~\eqref{eq:multi_layer_own_output_jacobian_bound} and the definition of the neighborhood envelopes, we have:
\begin{equation}
    \left\|\frac{\partial y_t^{(q)}}{\partial W^{(q)}}\right\|_F \leq L_{x,q}\left(H_{R_q} + M_{C,q}J_{R_q}\right).
\end{equation}

Then, by~\eqref{eq:multi_layer_K_temporal_sum_bound_uniform}
and the bound $\kappa_q(W) \leq M_{\kappa,q}$, for every
$t \in \{1,\dots,S\}$,
\begin{equation}
    \sum_{j=1}^{t} \left\|\frac{\partial y_t^{(q)}}{\partial x_j^{(q)}}\right\|_2 = \sum_{j=1}^{t}\left\|\mathbf K_{t,j}^{(q)}\right\|_2 \leq \kappa_q(W) \leq  M_{\kappa,q}.
\end{equation}

We next prove by induction on $q$ that
\begin{equation}\label{eq:multilayer_first_sensitivity_bound}
    \left\|Dy_t^{(q)}(W)[U]\right\|_2 \leq \Theta_q\left\|U^{(\leq q)}\right\|_F
\end{equation}
for every $q \in \{1,\dots,Q\}$ and every $t \in \{1,\dots,S\}$.

When $q=1$, the input sequence is independent of $W$, and the output of the first layer depends only on $W^{(1)}$. As such:
\begin{equation}
    Dy_t^{(1)}(W)[U] = \frac{\partial y_t^{(1)}}{\partial W^{(1)}}\operatorname{vec}\left(U^{(1)}\right),
\end{equation}
Using $\|Az\|_2\leq\|A\|_F\|z\|_2$ and $\|\operatorname{vec}(U^{(1)})\|_2=\|U^{(1)}\|_F$ gives
\begin{align}
    \left\|Dy_t^{(1)}(W)[U]\right\|_2 \leq L_{x,1}\left(H_{R_1}+M_{C,1}J_{R_1}\right)\left\|U^{(1)}\right\|_F=\Theta_1\left\|U^{(\leq1)}\right\|_F,
\end{align}
where the equality follows from $\Theta_0=0$ and the definition of $\Theta_1$.

Next, for a fixed $q \geq 2$, Since $U^{(\leq q-1)} = U^{(<q)}$, suppose (the induction hypothesis):
\begin{equation}\label{eq:multilayer_first_induction_hypothesis}
    \left\|Dy_j^{(q-1)}(W)[U]\right\|_2 \leq \Theta_{q-1}\left\|U^{(<q)}\right\|_F, \qquad j\in\{1,\dots,S\}.
\end{equation}

For a fixed $t$, the output $y_t^{(q)}$ depends on the current-layer parameters $W^{(q)}$ and the input tokens $x_1^{(q)}, \dots, x_t^{(q)}$, which depend on the preceding layers through $x_j^{(q)} = y_j^{(q-1)}$. The chain rule therefore gives
\begin{equation}
    Dy_t^{(q)}(W)[U] = \frac{\partial y_t^{(q)}}{\partial W^{(q)}} \operatorname{vec}\left(U^{(q)}\right) + \sum_{j=1}^{t}\frac{\partial y_t^{(q)}}{\partial x_j^{(q)}} Dy_j^{(q-1)}(W)[U].
\end{equation}
The first term accounts for the direct parameter perturbation in layer $q$, while the sum accounts for changes propagated through its input sequence.

Taking norms and applying the established Jacobian bounds and the induction hypothesis yields
\begin{align}
    \left\|Dy_t^{(q)}(W)[U]\right\|_2 \leq& L_{x,q}\left(H_{R_q} + M_{C,q}J_{R_q}\right)\left\|U^{(q)}\right\|_F + \sum_{j=1}^{t}\left\|\frac{\partial y_t^{(q)}}{\partial x_j^{(q)}}\right\|_2\left\|Dy_j^{(q-1)}(W)[U]\right\|_2 \nonumber \\
    \leq& L_{x,q}\left(H_{R_q} + M_{C,q}J_{R_q}\right)\left\|U^{(q)}\right\|_F + M_{\kappa,q}\Theta_{q-1}\left\|U^{(<q)}\right\|_F.
\end{align}
Applying Cauchy-Schwarz to the two terms gives
\begin{align}
    \left\|Dy_t^{(q)}(W)[U]\right\|_2 &\leq \left(L_{x,q}^2\left(H_{R_q} + M_{C,q}J_{R_q}\right)^2 + M_{\kappa,q}^2\Theta_{q-1}^2\right)^{1/2} \times \left(\left\|U^{(q)}\right\|_F^2 + \left\|U^{(<q)}\right\|_F^2\right)^{1/2} \nonumber \\ & = \Theta_q \left\|U^{(\leq q)}\right\|_F,
\end{align}
where the equality follows from the definition of $\Theta_q$ and the block Frobenius norm. This completes the induction.

The same argument applies to $V$. Finally, since $\|U^{(\leq q)}\|_F\leq\|U\|_F = 1$ and $\|V^{(\leq q)}\|_F\leq\|V\|_F=1$, we obtain the following first-derivative bounds:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:multilayer_first_sensitivity_unit}
    \left\|Dy_t^{(q)}(W)[U]\right\|_2\leq \Theta_q, \qquad \left\|Dy_t^{(q)}(W)[V]\right\|_2\leq \Theta_q,
\end{equation}
uniformly over all layers $q \in \{1,\dots,Q\}$ and token positions $t \in \{1,\dots,S\}$.

Finally, applying this result to $q = Q$, we get the resulting first-derivative bounds:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:Q_multilayer_first_sensitivity_unit}
    \left\|Dy_t^{(Q)}(W)[U]\right\|_2\leq \Theta_Q, \qquad \left\|Dy_t^{(Q)}(W)[V]\right\|_2\leq \Theta_Q,
\end{equation}
and substituting back into~\eqref{eq:multilayer_token_loss_hessian_reduction} we get: 
\begin{align}
    \Bigg|D^2\left(\ell_t\left(y_t^{(Q)}\right)\right)(W)[U,V]\Bigg| \leq & H_\ell \Theta_Q^2 + L_\ell \left\|D^2y_t^{(Q)}(W)[U,V]\right\|_2. \label{eq:multilayer_token_loss_hessian_reduction_with_P_Q_for_first_order_derivative}
\end{align}

We next bound the second output derivative term $\left\|D^2y_t^{(Q)}(W)[U,V]\right\|_2$. For any layer $q$, the second-order product rule gives:
\begin{align}
    D^2y_t^{(q)}(W)[U,V] =& \left(D^2h_t^{(q)}(W)[U,V]\right)^\top C_t^{(q)} + \left(h_t^{(q)}\right)^\top D^2C_t^{(q)}(W)[U,V] \nonumber\\
    &+ \left(Dh_t^{(q)}(W)[U]\right)^\top DC_t^{(q)}(W)[V] + \left(Dh_t^{(q)}(W)[V]\right)^\top DC_t^{(q)}(W)[U]. \label{eq:multilayer_output_second_derivative}
\end{align}
We bound each term separately. 

By the first-order bounds already established:
\begin{equation}
    \left\|Dx_t^{(q)}(W)[U]\right\|_2 = \left\|Dy_t^{(q-1)}(W)[U]\right\|_2 \leq \Theta_{q-1},
\end{equation}
vanishing for $q=1$, consistent with $\Theta_0 = 0$. The same holds for $\|Dx_t^{(q)}(W)[V]\|_2$. As a result, by the product rule:
\begin{align}
    \left\|DC_t^{(q)}(W)[U]\right\|_2 &\leq L_{x,q} + M_{C,q}\Theta_{q-1}, \\
    \left\|DC_t^{(q)}(W)[V]\right\|_2 &\leq L_{x,q} + M_{C,q}\Theta_{q-1},
    \\
    \left\|D^2C_t^{(q)}(W)[U,V]\right\|_2 &\leq 2\Theta_{q-1} + M_{C,q}\left\|D^2x_t^{(q)}(W)[U,V]\right\|_2.
\end{align}
Furthermore, by~\eqref{eq:multi_layer_state_jacobian_bound} and~\eqref{eq:final_D_ht_dxj_u},
\begin{align}
    \left\|Dh_t^{(q)}(W)[U]\right\|_F &\leq J_{R_q}  + M_{\Lambda_x,q}\Theta_{q-1} \sum_{j=1}^t\rho_q^{t-j} \leq J_{R_q} +\frac{M_{\Lambda_x,q}\Theta_{q-1}}{1 - \rho_q},
\end{align}
with the same inequality holding for $\left\|Dh_t^{(q)}(W)[V]\right\|_F$.

Since $\left\|C_t^{(q)}\right\|_2 \leq M_{C,q}L_{x,q}$ and $\left\|h_t^{(q)}\right\|_F\leq H_{R_q}$, equation~\eqref{eq:multilayer_output_second_derivative} yields
\begin{align}
    \left\|D^2y_t^{(q)}(W)[U,V]\right\|_2 \leq&  M_{C,q}L_{x,q}\left\|D^2h_t^{(q)}(W)[U,V]\right\|_F + M_{C,q}H_{R_q}\left\|D^2x_t^{(q)}(W)[U,V]\right\|_2 \nonumber\\
    &+ 2\left(L_{x,q} + M_{C,q}\Theta_{q-1}\right)\left(J_{R_q} +\frac{M_{\Lambda_x,q}\Theta_{q-1}}{1 - \rho_q}\right) + 2H_{R_q}\Theta_{q-1}.\label{eq:multilayer_output_second_reduction}
\end{align}

As a result, we need to bound the second derivative of the hidden state. By the definition of $h_t^{(q)}(W)$, differentiating it twice yields:
\begin{align}
    D^2h_t^{(q)}(W)[U,V] = & \bar A_t^{(q)}\odot D^2h_{t-1}^{(q)}(W)[U,V] + D^2\bar A_t^{(q)}(W)[U,V]\odot h_{t-1}^{(q)} \nonumber \\
    &+ D\bar A_t^{(q)}(W)[U] \odot Dh_{t-1}^{(q)}(W)[V] + D\bar A_t^{(q)}(W)[V]\odot Dh_{t-1}^{(q)}(W)[U] \nonumber \\ &+ D^2\left[B_t^{(q)}\left(\Delta_t^{(q)}\odot x_t^{(q)}\right)^\top\right](W)[U,V].
\end{align}

Then, since the absolute values of the first and second derivatives of $\operatorname{Softplus}$ are bounded above by $1$ and $1/4$, respectively, the product and chain rule give:
\begin{align}
    \left\|D\Delta_t^{(q)}(W)[U]\right\|_2 &\leq L_{x,q} + M_{\Delta,q}\Theta_{q-1}, \\
    \left\|D\Delta_t^{(q)}(W)[V]\right\|_2 &\leq L_{x,q} + M_{\Delta,q}\Theta_{q-1}, \\
    \left\|D^2\Delta_t^{(q)}(W)[U,V]\right\|_2 &\leq \frac{1}{4}\left(L_{x,q}+M_{\Delta,q}\Theta_{q-1}\right)^2 + 2\Theta_{q-1} + M_{\Delta,q}\left\|D^2x_t^{(q)}(W)[U,V]\right\|_2.
\end{align}

Then, applying the product rule and the exponential chain rule (per every entry of $A$), and using the established state and first-derivative bounds, we get:
\begin{align}
    \left\|D^2h_t^{(q)}(W)[U,V]\right\|_F \leq & \rho_q\left\|D^2h_{t-1}^{(q)}(W)[U,V]\right\|_F + \mathcal E_q + M_{\Lambda_x,q} \left\|D^2x_t^{(q)}(W)[U,V]\right\|_2, \label{eq:multilayer_second_state_recurrence}
\end{align}
where we collected all the terms independent of the second input derivative in $\mathcal E_q$ defined as
\begin{align}
    \mathcal E_q :=& \rho_q H_{R_q} \Bigg[\left(M_{K_\Delta,q} + M_{A,q}\left(L_{x,q} + M_{\Delta,q}\Theta_{q-1}\right)\right)^2 + 2\left(L_{x,q} + M_{\Delta,q}\Theta_{q-1}\right) \nonumber \\
    &+ M_{A,q}\left(\frac{1}{4}\left(L_{x,q} + M_{\Delta,q}\Theta_{q-1}\right)^2 + 2\Theta_{q-1}\right)\Bigg] \nonumber \\
    &+ 2\rho_q\left(M_{K_\Delta,q} + M_{A,q}\left(L_{x,q} + M_{\Delta,q}\Theta_{q-1}\right)\right)\left(J_{R_q} + \frac{M_{\Lambda_x,q}\Theta_{q-1}}{1-\rho_q}\right)\nonumber \\
    & + 2\Theta_{q-1}M_{K_\Delta,q}L_{x,q}\nonumber \\
    & + 2\left(L_{x,q} + M_{B,q}\Theta_{q-1}\right)\left[L_{x,q}\left(L_{x,q} + M_{\Delta,q}\Theta_{q-1}\right) + M_{K_\Delta,q}\Theta_{q-1}\right] \nonumber \\
    & + M_{B,q}L_{x,q}\Bigg[L_{x,q}\left(\frac{1}{4}\left(L_{x,q} + M_{\Delta,q}\Theta_{q-1}\right)^2 + 2\Theta_{q-1}\right) + 2\left(L_{x,q} + M_{\Delta,q}\Theta_{q-1}\right)\Theta_{q-1}\Bigg].\label{eq:multilayer_second_state_forcing}
\end{align}
The first term in $\mathcal E_q$ comes from the second transition derivative multiplied entrywise by the state:
\begin{equation*}
    D^2\bar A_t^{(q)}(W)[U,V]\odot h_{t-1}^{(q)}.
\end{equation*}
The second comes from the two mixed transition-state terms,
\begin{equation*}
    D\bar A_t^{(q)}(W)[U]\odot Dh_{t-1}^{(q)}(W)[V] + D\bar A_t^{(q)}(W)[V]\odot Dh_{t-1}^{(q)}(W)[U].
\end{equation*}
The remaining three terms come from differentiating the
additive state term
\begin{equation*}
    B_t^{(q)}\left(\Delta_t^{(q)}\odot x_t^{(q)}\right)^\top
\end{equation*}
twice. The contributions involving the second input derivative
are collectively bounded by
\begin{equation*}
    M_{\Lambda_x,q}\left\|D^2x_t^{(q)}(W)[U,V]\right\|_2,
\end{equation*}
where $M_{\Lambda_x,q}$ was defined in~\eqref{eq:multi_layer_M_Lambda_x}.

Since the initial state and its derivatives equal $\mathbf{0}$, unrolling~\eqref{eq:multilayer_second_state_recurrence} and summing the geometric series gives
\begin{equation}
    \left\|D^2h_t^{(q)}(W)[U,V]\right\|_F \leq \frac{\mathcal E_q}{1-\rho_q} + \frac{M_{\Lambda_x,q}}{1-\rho_q}\max_{1\leq j\leq t}\left\|D^2x_j^{(q)}(W)[U,V]\right\|_2.\label{eq:multilayer_second_state_bound}
\end{equation}

Next, we note that since $\left\|D^2x_t^{(q)}(W)[U,V]\right\|_2 \leq \max_{1\leq j\leq t}\left\|D^2x_j^{(q)}(W)[U,V]\right\|_2$, substituting~\eqref{eq:multilayer_second_state_bound} and using this fact, the new coefficient of the $\max_{1\leq j\leq t}\left\|D^2x_j^{(q)}(W)[U,V]\right\|_2$ term becomes:
\begin{equation}
    M_{C,q}H_{R_q} + \frac{M_{C,q}L_{x,q}M_{\Lambda_x,q}}{1-\rho_q} = M_{\kappa,q}.
\end{equation}

Finally, we define a new recursive term $\Omega_q$ as follows:
\begin{align}
    \Omega_0 &:= 0, \nonumber \\
    \Omega_q &:= \frac{M_{C,q}L_{x,q}\mathcal E_q}{1 - \rho_q} + 2\left(L_{x,q}+M_{C,q}\Theta_{q-1}\right)\left(J_{R_q} + \frac{M_{\Lambda_x,q}\Theta_{q-1}}{1-\rho_q}\right) \nonumber \\ &\qquad + 2H_{R_q}\Theta_{q-1} + M_{\kappa,q}\Omega_{q-1}. \label{eq:multilayer_second_sensitivity_recursion}
\end{align}
For $q=1$, second input derivative vanishes. Then, for $q \geq 2$, and using the similar induction argument as in the first-order derivative, as well as the fact $x_j^{(q)}=y_j^{(q-1)}$, the induction yields:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:multilayer_second_sensitivity_bound}
    \left\|D^2y_t^{(q)}(W)[U,V]\right\|_2\leq\Omega_q
\end{equation}
for every layer and token position.
All constants are finite and independent of $W$, $\xi$, $S$, and $t$, because the neighborhood envelopes are fixed, $\rho_q<1$, and $Q$ is finite.

Finally, applying this bound to $q=Q$, and substituting the result into~\eqref{eq:multilayer_token_loss_hessian_reduction} it follows that:
\begin{equation}
    \Bigg|D^2\left(\ell_t\left(y_t^{(Q)}\right)\right)(W)[U,V]
    \Bigg|
    \leq H_\ell\Theta_Q^2+L_\ell\Omega_Q =: L_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf R_Q}^{(Q)}.
\end{equation}

Averaging over the token losses preserves this bound. Since $U,V$ are arbitrary unit directions, it is true that:
\begin{equation}
    \left\|\nabla_W^2\mathcal L^{S,(Q)}(W;\xi) \right\|_{\mathrm{op}} \leq L_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf R_Q}^{(Q)},
\end{equation}
where the operator norm is induced by the block Frobenius norm.

Since $\mathcal N_{\mathbf R_Q}$ is convex, integrating the Hessian along the segment joining any $W_0, W_1 \in\mathcal N_{\mathbf R_Q}$ gives
\begin{equation}
    \left\|\nabla_W\mathcal L^{S,(Q)}(W_1;\xi) - \nabla_W\mathcal L^{S,(Q)}(W_0;\xi)\right\|_F \leq L_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf R_Q}^{(Q)}\|W_1-W_0\|_F.
\end{equation}

Taking expectations, by Assumption~\ref{assum:unbiased_stochastic_gradients} and Jensen's inequality, we get:
\begin{align}
    \left\|\nabla_W\mathcal L_k(W_1) - \nabla_W\mathcal L_k(W_0)\right\|_F &= \left\|\mathbb E_{\xi \sim \mathcal P_k}\left[\nabla_W\mathcal L^{S,(Q)}(W_1;\xi) - \nabla_W\mathcal L^{S,(Q)}(W_0;\xi)\right]\right\|_F \nonumber \\ 
    &\leq \mathbb E_{\xi \sim \mathcal P_k}\left[\left\|\nabla_W\mathcal L^{S,(Q)}(W_1;\xi) - \nabla_W\mathcal L^{S,(Q)}(W_0;\xi)\right\|_F\right] \nonumber \\ &\leq L_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf R_Q}^{(Q)}\|W_1-W_0\|_F,
\end{align}
demonstrating that every client objective is $L_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf R_Q}^{(Q)}$-smooth on $\mathcal N_{\mathbf R_Q}$.

Finally, since this inequality holds for every client objective, and $p_k\geq0$ and $\sum_kp_k=1$, it also holds for $f = \sum_kp_k\mathcal L_k$, demonstrating that $f$ is also $L_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf R_Q}^{(Q)}$-smooth on $\mathcal N_{\mathbf R_Q}$, completing the proof. \qed
<!-- end head-9 -->
<!-- lemma-7: body_md -->
\label{lem:multilayer_ssm_fedavg_g_constant} 
Suppose the Assumptions~\ref{assum:lipschitz_cont_loss} and \ref{assum:bounded_layer_inuts} hold. Let $\mathcal N_{\mathbf{R}_Q}(W^r) \subseteq \mathcal S^{(Q)}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\delta}}$ be a stable local model neighborhood around the round-$r$ broadcast $Q$-deep model $W^r$. Let:
\begin{equation}
    g(W;\xi) := \nabla_W\mathcal L^{S,(Q)}(W;\xi)
\end{equation}
denote the stochastic sequence-level gradient for a sampled input-target sequence pair $\xi = (x_{1:S}, y_{1:S}^*)$. Then, for every $W \in \mathcal N_{\mathbf{R}_Q}(W^r)$,
\begin{equation}
    \mathbb E_{\xi} \left\|g(W;\xi)\right\|_F^2 \leq \left(G_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf{R}_Q}^{(Q)}\right)^2,
\end{equation}
where
\begin{equation}
    G_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf{R}_Q}^{(Q)} := L_\ell \left  (\sum_{q=1}^Q L_{x,q}^2\left(H_{R_q} + M_{C,q}J_{R_q}\right)^2 \prod _{q'=q+1}^Q M_{\kappa,{q'}}^2\right)^{1/2} = L_\ell\Theta_Q.
\end{equation}
where $H_{R_q}, J_{R_q}, M_{C,q} \text{ and } M_{\kappa,q}$ are defined in~\eqref{eq:multi_layer_H_R_definition},~\eqref{eq:multi_layer_J_R_definition},~\eqref{eq:multi_layer_parameter_envelopes}, and ~\eqref{eq:multi_layer_M_kappa} respectively.
<!-- end lemma-7 -->
<!-- head-10: body_md -->
By Theorem~\ref{thm:ssm_multi_layer_grad_bound}, under the assumptions of the lemma, for every $W \in \mathcal N_{\mathbf{R}_Q}(W^r) \subseteq \mathcal S^{(Q)}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\delta}}$, every finite integer $S \geq 1$, and every admissible length-$S$ sequence pair $\xi \in \mathcal Z$, we have:
\begin{align}
    \left\|\nabla_W \mathcal L^{S,(Q)}(W;\xi)\right\|_F \leq  L_\ell \left  (\sum_{q=1}^Q L_{x,q}^2\left(H_{q} + \left\|W_C^{(q)}\right\|_2J_{q}\right)^2 \prod _{q'=q+1}^Q \kappa_{q'}^2\right)^{1/2}. \nonumber
\end{align}
Since $g(W;\xi) = \nabla_W\mathcal L^{S,(Q)}(W;\xi)$, it follows that:
\begin{equation}
    \|g(W;\xi)\|_F \leq  L_\ell \left  (\sum_{q=1}^Q L_{x,q}^2\left(H_{q} + \left\|W_C^{(q)}\right\|_2J_{q}\right)^2 \prod _{q'=q+1}^Q \kappa_{q'}^2\right)^{1/2}.
\end{equation}
Restricting to $W  \in \mathcal N_{\mathbf{R}_Q}(W^r)$, by the local neighborhood bounds defined previously (~\eqref{eq:multi_layer_H_R_definition},~\eqref{eq:multi_layer_J_R_definition},~\eqref{eq:multi_layer_parameter_envelopes}, and ~\eqref{eq:multi_layer_M_kappa}), 
\begin{align}
    H_q\leq H_{R_q},
    \qquad
    \left\|W_C^{(q)}\right\|_2 \leq M_{C,q},
    \qquad
    J_q\leq J_{R_q},
    \qquad
    \kappa_q \leq M_{\kappa,q}.
\end{align}

Therefore, 
\begin{equation}\label{eq:multilayer_g_ssm_definition}
    \left\|g(W;\xi)\right\|_F \leq L_\ell \left  (\sum_{q=1}^Q L_{x,q}^2\left(H_{R_q} + M_{C,q}J_{R_q}\right)^2 \prod _{q'=q+1}^Q M_{\kappa,{q'}}^2\right)^{1/2} = G_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf{R}_Q}^{(Q)}.
\end{equation}
Since both values are non-negative, squaring both sides gives:
\begin{equation}
    \|g(W;\xi)\|_F^2 \leq \left(G_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf{R}_Q}^{(Q)}\right)^2 = L_\ell^2 \left  (\sum_{q=1}^Q L_{x,q}^2\left(H_{R_q} + M_{C,q}J_{R_q}\right)^2 \prod _{q'=q+1}^Q M_{\kappa,{q'}}^2\right)
\end{equation}

Finally, taking expectation over the sampled sequence $\xi$ preserves the bound:
\begin{equation}
    \mathbb E_\xi\left[\|g(W;\xi)\|_F^2\right] \leq \left(G_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf{R}_Q}^{(Q)}\right)^2
\end{equation}
proving the lemma. \qed
<!-- end head-10 -->
<!-- lemma-8: body_md -->
\label{lem:multilayer_ssm_variance_bound}
Suppose the assumptions of Lemma~\ref{lem:multilayer_ssm_fedavg_g_constant} and Assumption~\ref{assum:unbiased_stochastic_gradients} hold. Let
\begin{equation*}
    \mathcal L(W):=\mathbb E_{\xi\sim\mathcal P}\left[\mathcal L^{S, (Q)}(W;\xi)\right]
\end{equation*}
denote the expected sequence loss over some local data distribution $\mathcal P$, for a $Q$-layer deep SSM parameterized by $W$. Define
\begin{equation*}
    g(W;\xi):=\nabla_W\mathcal L^{S, (Q)}(W;\xi)
\end{equation*}
as the single-sequence stochastic gradient. For a mini-batch
$\Xi_\xi=\{\xi_1,\dots,\xi_\Xi\}$ of $\Xi$ independent sampled sequences, define
\begin{equation}
    g_\Xi(W;\Xi_\xi) := \frac{1}{\Xi}\sum_{j=1}^\Xi g(W;\xi_j).
\end{equation}
Then, for every $W\in\mathcal N_{\mathbf{R}_Q}(W^r)$ it follows that
\begin{equation}
    \mathbb E_{\Xi_\xi}\left[\left\|g_\Xi(W;\Xi_\xi)-\nabla_W\mathcal L(W)\right\|_F^2\right] \leq \left(\sigma_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf{R}_Q,\Xi}^{(Q)}\right)^2,
\end{equation}
where
\begin{equation}
   \left(\sigma_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf{R}_Q,\Xi}^{(Q)}\right)^2 := \frac{1}{\Xi}\sup_{W\in\mathcal N_{\mathbf R_Q}(W^r)}\left(\mathbb E_{\xi\sim\mathcal P}\|g(W;\xi)\|_F^2 - \|\nabla_W\mathcal L(W)\|_F^2\right). 
\end{equation}
Moreover, by Lemma~\ref{lem:multilayer_ssm_fedavg_g_constant},
\begin{equation}
    \left(\sigma_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf{R}_Q,\Xi}^{(Q)}\right)^2 \leq \frac{\left(G_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf{R}_Q}^{(Q)}\right)^2}{\Xi}.
\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-8 -->
<!-- head-11: body_md -->
By unbiasedness and independence of the sampled sequences, the standard mini-batch variance identity gives:
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb E_{\Xi_\xi}\left[\left\|g_\Xi(W;\Xi_\xi)-\nabla_W\mathcal L(W)\right\|_F^2\right] = \frac{1}{\Xi}\mathbb E_{\xi\sim\mathcal P}\left[\left\|g(W;\xi)-\nabla_W\mathcal L(W)\right\|_F^2\right].
\end{align*}
Using the identity
\begin{equation}
    \mathbb E_{\xi\sim\mathcal P} \left[\left\|g(W;\xi)-\nabla_W\mathcal L(W)\right\|_F^2\right] = \mathbb E_{\xi\sim\mathcal P} \|g(W;\xi)\|_F^2 - \|\nabla_W\mathcal L(W)\|_F^2,
\end{equation}
and taking the supremum over $W\in \mathcal N_{\mathbf{R}_Q}(W^r)$, we obtain
\begin{equation}
    \mathbb E_{\Xi_\xi}\left[\left\|g_\Xi(W;\Xi_\xi)-\nabla_W\mathcal L(W)\right\|_F^2\right] \leq \left(\sigma_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf{R}_Q,\Xi}^{(Q)}\right)^2.
\end{equation}

Finally, Lemma~\ref{lem:multilayer_ssm_fedavg_g_constant} gives
\begin{equation*}
    \mathbb E_{\xi\sim\mathcal P}\|g(W;\xi)\|_F^2 \leq \left(G_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf{R}_Q}^{(Q)}\right)^2
\end{equation*}
and since $\|\nabla_W\mathcal L(W)\|_F^2\geq 0$,
\begin{equation}
    \left(\sigma_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf{R}_Q,\Xi}^{(Q)}\right)^2 \leq \frac{\left(G_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf{R}_Q}^{(Q)}\right)^2}{\Xi}.
\end{equation}
This proves the claim. \qed
<!-- end head-11 -->
<!-- lemma-9: body_md -->
\label{lem:multilayer_ssm_gamma_tv_bound}
Suppose the assumptions of Lemma~\ref{lem:multilayer_ssm_fedavg_g_constant} and Assumption~\ref{assum:unbiased_stochastic_gradients} hold. Let
\begin{equation}
    f(W) := \sum_{k=1}^K p_k \mathcal L_k(W)
\end{equation}
be the global objective, where $p_k \geq 0$ and $\sum_{k=1}^K p_k=1$. Let $\mathcal P_k$ denote the local data distribution of client $k$, and define the global mixture distribution as:
\begin{equation}
    \bar{\mathcal P} := \sum_{k=1}^K p_k \mathcal P_k.
\end{equation}
Define the local client heterogeneity constant
\begin{equation}
    \left(\Gamma_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf{R}_Q}^{(Q)}\right)^2:=\sup_{W \in \mathcal N_{\mathbf{R}_Q}(W^r)}\sum_{k=1}^K p_k \left\|\nabla_W \mathcal L_k(W) - \nabla_W f(W) \right\|_F^2.
\end{equation}
Then:
\begin{equation}
    \left(\Gamma_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf{R}_Q}^{(Q)}\right)^2 \leq 4\left(G_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf{R}_Q}^{(Q)}\right)^2 \sum_{k=1}^K p_k \operatorname{TV}(\mathcal{P}_k, \bar{\mathcal P})^2,
\end{equation}
where
\begin{equation}
    \operatorname{TV}(P,Q):=\sup_A |P(A)-Q(A)|.
\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-9 -->
<!-- head-12: body_md -->
Recall that for each client $k$, the local objective is:
\begin{equation*}
    \mathcal L_k(W) =  \mathbb E_{\xi \sim \mathcal P_k}\left[\mathcal L^{S,(Q)}(W;\xi)\right],
\end{equation*}
and, by Assumption~\ref{assum:unbiased_stochastic_gradients},
\begin{align}
    \nabla_W\mathcal L_k(W) = \mathbb E_{\xi\sim \mathcal P_k}\left[g(W;\xi)\right].
\end{align}
Similarly, since
\begin{equation*}
    f(W) := \sum_{k=1}^K p_k \mathcal L_k(W),
\end{equation*}
we have
\begin{equation}
    \nabla_W f(W) = \sum_{k=1}^Kp_k\nabla_W\mathcal L_k(W) = \sum_{k=1}^Kp_k\mathbb E_{\xi\sim \mathcal P_k}\left[g(W;\xi)\right].
\end{equation}

Next, by the definition of the mixture distribution $\bar{\mathcal P}$, we can write this as:
\begin{equation}
     \nabla_W f(W) = \mathbb E_{\xi \sim \bar{\mathcal P}}\left[g(W;\xi)\right].
\end{equation}
Therefore, it follows that
\begin{equation}
    \nabla_W\mathcal L_k(W)-\nabla_W f(W) = \mathbb E_{\xi\sim \mathcal P_k}[g(W;\xi)] - \mathbb E_{\xi\sim \bar{\mathcal P}}[g(W;\xi)].
\end{equation}

By the pointwise bound~\eqref{eq:multilayer_g_ssm_definition} established in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:multilayer_ssm_fedavg_g_constant}, for every $W \in \mathcal N_{\mathbf{R}_Q}(W^r)$ and every admissible sequence pair $\xi \in \mathcal Z$,
\begin{equation*}
    \left\|g(W;\xi)\right\|_F \leq G_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf{R}_Q}^{(Q)}.
\end{equation*}
Since $\mathcal P_k(\mathcal Z) = \bar{\mathcal P}(\mathcal Z) = 1$, the total-variation inequality for bounded vector-valued functions gives:
\begin{equation}
    \left\|\mathbb E_{\xi\sim \mathcal P_k}[g(W;\xi)] - \mathbb E_{\xi\sim \bar{\mathcal P}}[g(W;\xi)]\right\|_F \leq 2G_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf{R}_Q}^{(Q)}\operatorname{TV}(\mathcal P_k,\bar{\mathcal P}).
\end{equation}
So:
\begin{equation}
    \left\|\nabla_W\mathcal L_k(W)-\nabla_W f(W)\right\|_F^2 \leq 4\left(G_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf{R}_Q}^{(Q)}\right)^2\operatorname{TV}(\mathcal P_k,\bar{\mathcal P})^2.
\end{equation}

Multiplying both sides by $p_k$ and summing over all clients gives:
\begin{equation}
    \sum_{k=1}^K p_k \left\|\nabla_W\mathcal L_k(W)-\nabla_W f(W) \right\|_F^2 \leq 4\left(G_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf{R}_Q}^{(Q)}\right)^2\sum_{k=1}^K p_k \operatorname{TV}(\mathcal P_k,\bar{\mathcal P})^2.
\end{equation} 

Since the right-hand side of this inequality is independent of $W$, taking the supremum over $W \in \mathcal N_{\mathbf{R}_Q}(W^r)$ we get:
\begin{equation}
    \left(\Gamma_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf{R}_Q}^{(Q)}\right)^2 := \sup_{W \in \mathcal N_{\mathbf{R}_Q}(W^r)} \sum_{k=1}^K p_k\left\|\nabla_W \mathcal L_k(W)-\nabla_W f(W)\right\|_F^2 \leq 4\left(G_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf{R}_Q}^{(Q)}\right)^2\sum_{k=1}^Kp_k \operatorname{TV}(\mathcal P_k,\bar{\mathcal P})^2.
\end{equation}

This proves the multi-layer SSM client heterogeneity bound. \qed
<!-- end head-12 -->
<!-- corollary-2: body_md -->
\label{thm:multilayer_fedavg_ssm_convergence}

Consider the distributed learning framework established in Section~\ref{sec:system_model}, with objective
\begin{equation*}
    f(W):=\sum_{k=1}^K p_k\mathcal L_k(W), \qquad p_k\geq0, \qquad \sum_{k=1}^K p_k=1,
\end{equation*}
where the weights $p_k$ are fixed and
\begin{equation*}
        W=\left(W^{(1)},\ldots,W^{(Q)}\right)
\end{equation*}
parameterizes the selective SSM of finite depth $Q\geq1$ defined in Appendix~\ref{appendix:multi_layer_selective_ssm}.

Each client objective is finite and differentiable on the neighborhoods under consideration, and satisfies
\begin{equation*}
    \nabla_W\mathcal L_k(W) = \mathbb E_{\xi\sim\mathcal P_k} \left[\nabla_W\mathcal L^{S,(Q)}(W;\xi)\right].
\end{equation*}
where $S$ is the length of the sampled sequence pair $\xi$, and all clients use the same sequence-loss map.

Let $W^0$ be deterministic with $f(W^0)<\infty$, and suppose that $f$ is bounded below by a finite constant $f^*$.

Consider full-participation FedAvg for $T$ communication rounds, with $E$ local steps per round, mini-batch size $\Xi$, and constant deterministic local step size $\eta>0$, where $T,E,\Xi \in \mathbb Z_{>0}$:
\begin{align*}
    W_k^{r,0} &= W^r,\\
    W_k^{r,e+1} &= W_k^{r,e}-\eta g_{k,\Xi}^{r,e}, \qquad e=0,\ldots,E-1,\\
    W^{r+1} & =\sum_{k=1}^K p_kW_k^{r,E}.
\end{align*}
The mini-batch gradient is
\begin{equation*}
    g_{k,\Xi}^{r,e} := \frac{1}{\Xi}\sum_{i=1}^{\Xi} \nabla_W\mathcal L^{S_i,(Q)}(W_k^{r,e};\xi_{k,i}^{r,e}),
\end{equation*}
where $S_i$ denotes the length of $\xi_{k,i}^{r,e}$.

Conditional on the complete training history before each local step, each client draws $\Xi$ fresh independent samples from $\mathcal P_k$, and the mini-batches drawn by different clients are conditionally independent.

For fixed deterministic radii $R_q > 0$, define
\begin{align*}
    \mathcal N_{\mathbf R_Q}(W^r) := \prod_{q=1}^Q \mathcal N_{R_q}^{(q)}(W^{r,(q)}), \qquad \mathbf R_Q:=(R_1,\ldots,R_Q).
\end{align*}
    
Suppose that, almost surely, for every round $r<T$, client $k$, and local step $0\leq e\leq E$,
\begin{equation*}
    W_k^{r,e} \in \mathcal N_{\mathbf R_Q}(W^r) \subseteq \mathcal S_{\boldsymbol\gamma,\boldsymbol\delta}^{(Q)}.
\end{equation*}
Suppose also that finite deterministic envelopes $M_{X,q}$ satisfy, almost surely for every round,
\begin{equation*}
    \left\|W_X^{r,(q)}\right \|_2+R_q \leq M_{X,q}, \qquad X\in\{\Delta,B,C,A\}, \qquad q\in\{1,\ldots,Q\},
\end{equation*}
where $W_A^{r,(q)}:=A^{r,(q)}$.

The stability margins $\gamma_q,\delta_q>0$, the layer-input bounds $L_{x,q}$, the loss-regularity constants $L_\ell,H_\ell$, and the parameter envelopes $M_{X,q}$ are deterministic and common across rounds.

Let Assumption~\ref{assum:bounded_layer_inuts} hold uniformly on all these neighborhoods, and for each layer, define
\begin{align*}
    \rho_q:=\exp(-\gamma_q\delta_q)<1.   
\end{align*}
Define:
\begin{align*}
    M_{K_\Delta, q} & := M_{\Delta,q} L_{x,q} + \sqrt{D}\log(2), \\ M_{K_B,q} &:= M_{B,q}L_{x,q}^2, \\
    M_{K_W,q} &:= ND M_{K_{B},q}M_{K_{\Delta},q}\left(M_{K_{\Delta},q} + \sqrt{D} M_{A,q}L_{x,q}\right)
     \left(\frac{\rho_q}{1-\rho_q}\right) \\ &\qquad + L_{x,q}^2 \sqrt{N} M_{K_{\Delta},q} + L_{x,q} \sqrt{D} M_{K_B,q}, \\
     H_{R_q} &:= \frac{M_{K_B,q} M_{K_{\Delta},q}
    \sqrt{ND}}{1-\rho_q}, \\ 
    J_{R_q} &:= \frac{M_{K_W,q}}{1-\rho_q},  \\
    M_{\Lambda_x,q} &:= \rho_q M_{A,q}M_{\Delta,q}H_{R_q} + M_{B,q}L_{x,q}\left(2M_{K_\Delta,q} + L_{x,q}M_{\Delta,q}\right), \\
    M_{\kappa,q} &:= M_{C,q}\left(H_{R_q} + \frac{L_{x,q}M_{\Lambda_x,q}}{1-\rho_q}\right),\\
    \Theta_q &: =\left(L_{x,q}^2\left(H_{R_q}+M_{C,q}J_{R_q}\right)^2+M_{\kappa,q}^2\Theta_{q-1}^2\right)^{1/2}, \qquad \Theta_0 := 0,  \\
    \mathcal E_q :=& \rho_q H_{R_q} \Bigg[\left(M_{K_\Delta,q} + M_{A,q}\left(L_{x,q} + M_{\Delta,q}\Theta_{q-1}\right)\right)^2 + 2\left(L_{x,q} + M_{\Delta,q}\Theta_{q-1}\right) \nonumber \\
    &+ M_{A,q}\left(\frac{1}{4}\left(L_{x,q} + M_{\Delta,q}\Theta_{q-1}\right)^2 + 2\Theta_{q-1}\right)\Bigg] \nonumber \\
    &+ 2\rho_q\left(M_{K_\Delta,q} + M_{A,q}\left(L_{x,q} + M_{\Delta,q}\Theta_{q-1}\right)\right)\left(J_{R_q} + \frac{M_{\Lambda_x,q}\Theta_{q-1}}{1-\rho_q}\right)\nonumber \\
    & + 2\Theta_{q-1}M_{K_\Delta,q}L_{x,q}\nonumber \\
    & + 2\left(L_{x,q} + M_{B,q}\Theta_{q-1}\right)\left[L_{x,q}\left(L_{x,q} + M_{\Delta,q}\Theta_{q-1}\right) + M_{K_\Delta,q}\Theta_{q-1}\right] \nonumber \\
    & + M_{B,q}L_{x,q}\Bigg[L_{x,q}\left(\frac{1}{4}\left(L_{x,q} + M_{\Delta,q}\Theta_{q-1}\right)^2 + 2\Theta_{q-1}\right) + 2\left(L_{x,q} + M_{\Delta,q}\Theta_{q-1}\right)\Theta_{q-1}\Bigg],\\
    \Omega_q &:= \frac{M_{C,q}L_{x,q}\mathcal E_q}{1 - \rho_q} + 2\left(L_{x,q}+M_{C,q}\Theta_{q-1}\right)\left(J_{R_q} + \frac{M_{\Lambda_x,q}\Theta_{q-1}}{1-\rho_q}\right) + 2H_{R_q}\Theta_{q-1} + M_{\kappa,q}\Omega_{q-1}, \\ \Omega_0 &= 0
\end{align*}

Let:
\begin{align*}
    L_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf R_Q}^{(Q)} &:= H_\ell\Theta_Q^2+L_\ell\Omega_Q,\\
    G_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf R_Q}^{(Q)} &:= L_\ell\Theta_Q = L_\ell \left(\sum_{q=1}^Q L_{x,q}^2\left(H_{R_q}+M_{C,q}J_{R_q}\right)^2\prod_{q'=q+1}^Q M_{\kappa,q'}^2\right)^{1/2},
\end{align*}
define the common deterministic smoothness and gradient bounds on all the considered neighborhoods.

For the client distributions, define
\begin{equation*}
    \bar{\mathcal P} := \sum_{k=1}^Kp_k\mathcal P_k, \qquad \mathcal H_{\mathrm{TV}} := \sum_{k=1}^Kp_k \operatorname{TV}(\mathcal P_k,\bar{\mathcal P})^2,
\end{equation*}
where
\begin{equation*}
    \operatorname{TV}(P,Q) := \sup_{\mathcal A}|P(\mathcal A)-Q(\mathcal A)|.
\end{equation*}

If the step size satisfies
\begin{equation*}
    \eta E L_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf R_Q}^{(Q)} \leq\frac{1}{2}, \qquad 12\eta^2\left(L_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf R_Q}^{(Q)}\right)^2E(E-1) \leq 1, 
\end{equation*}
then
\begin{align*}
    \frac{1}{T}\sum_{r=0}^{T-1} \mathbb E\|\nabla f(W^r)\|_F^2 \leq& \frac{4(f(W^0)-f^*)}{\eta ET} + \frac{4\eta L_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf R_Q}^{(Q)}\left(G_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf R_Q}^{(Q)}\right)^2}{\Xi} \sum_{k=1}^Kp_k^2 \nonumber \\
    &+ \frac{6\eta^2\left(L_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf R_Q}^{(Q)}\right)^2(E-1)\left(G_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf R_Q}^{(Q)}\right)^2}{\Xi} \nonumber \\ &+ 48\eta^2\left(L_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf R_Q}^{(Q)}\right)^2E(E-1)\left(G_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf R_Q}^{(Q)}\right)^2\mathcal H_{\mathrm{TV}}.
\end{align*}
<!-- end corollary-2 -->
<!-- head-13: body_md -->
We apply the federated optimization argument from the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:fedavg_ssm_convergence} to the full parameter tuple $W=\left(W^{(1)},\ldots,W^{(Q)}\right)$. After vectorization, the Euclidean norm of this tuple is exactly its block Frobenius norm.

The preceding neighborhood estimates remain valid when the reference-dependent parameter envelopes are replaced by the common deterministic upper envelopes $M_{X,q}$, because their defining expressions are nondecreasing in those envelopes.

Lemma~\ref{lem:multilayer_local_smoothness} therefore supplies the common smoothness constant $L=L_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf R_Q}^{(Q)}$. 

Lemma~\ref{lem:multilayer_ssm_variance_bound}, applied conditionally on the history before each mini-batch,
supplies the common variance bound $\sigma^2 = \frac{\left(G_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf R_Q}^{(Q)}\right)^2}{\Xi}.$

Conditional independence across clients gives the corresponding weighted-noise variance bound $\sigma^2\sum_kp_k^2$.

Lemma~\ref{lem:multilayer_ssm_gamma_tv_bound} supplies the common client-gradient disagreement bound $\Gamma^2 = 4\left(G_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf R_Q}^{(Q)}\right)^2\mathcal H_{\mathrm{TV}}.$

Each neighborhood $\mathcal N_{\mathbf R_Q}(W^r)$ is a product of convex balls. Since it contains all local iterates in round $r$, it also contains their weighted aggregate and the segments used in the smoothness argument.

Substituting these constants into \eqref{eq:generic_fedavg_bound_appendix} yields the desired inequality in Corollary~\ref{thm:multilayer_fedavg_ssm_convergence} under the specified step size conditions.
\qed
<!-- end head-13 -->
<!-- corollary-3: body_md -->
\label{thm:multilayer_fedprox_ssm_convergence}

Consider the distributed learning framework established in Section~\ref{sec:system_model}, with objective
\begin{equation*}
    f(W) := \sum_{k=1}^K p_k\mathcal L_k(W), \qquad p_k \geq 0, \qquad \sum_{k=1}^K p_k = 1,
\end{equation*}
where the weights $p_k$ are fixed and $W = \left(W^{(1)},\ldots,W^{(Q)}\right)$ parameterizes the selective SSM of finite integer depth $Q \geq 1$ defined in Appendix~\ref{appendix:multi_layer_selective_ssm}.

Each client objective is finite and differentiable on the neighborhoods under consideration and satisfies
\begin{equation*}
    \nabla_W\mathcal L_k(W) =\mathbb E_{\xi\sim\mathcal P_k} \left[\nabla_W\mathcal L^{S,(Q)}(W;\xi)\right],
\end{equation*}
where $S$ is the length of the sampled sequence pair $\xi$, and all clients use the same sequence-loss map. Let $W^0$ be deterministic with $f(W^0) < \infty$, and suppose that $f$ is bounded below by a finite constant $f^*$.

Consider full-participation FedProx for $T$ communication rounds, with $E$ local steps per round, mini-batch size $\Xi$, constant deterministic local step size $\eta > 0$, and constant deterministic proximal coefficient $\mu > 0$, where $T, E, \Xi \in \mathbb Z_{>0}$:
\begin{align*}
    W_k^{r,0} &= W^r,\\
    W_k^{r,e+1} &= W_k^{r,e} - \eta\left(g_{k,\Xi}^{r,e} + \mu(W_k^{r,e}-W^r)\right), \qquad e=0,\ldots,E-1,\\
    W^{r+1} &= \sum_{k=1}^K p_kW_k^{r,E}, \qquad r=0,\ldots,T-1.
\end{align*}
The mini-batch gradient is
\begin{equation*}
    g_{k,\Xi}^{r,e} := \frac{1}{\Xi}\sum_{i=1}^{\Xi} \nabla_W\mathcal L^{S_i,(Q)}\left(W_k^{r,e};\xi_{k,i}^{r,e}\right),
\end{equation*}
where $S_i$ denotes the length of $\xi_{k,i}^{r,e}$. Conditional on the complete training history before each local step, each client draws $\Xi$ fresh independent samples from $\mathcal P_k$, and the mini-batches drawn by different clients are conditionally independent.

For fixed deterministic radii $R_q > 0$, define
\begin{equation*}
    \mathcal N_{\mathbf R_Q}(W^r) := \prod_{q=1}^Q \mathcal N_{R_q}^{(q)}(W^{r,(q)}), \qquad \mathbf R_Q:=(R_1,\ldots,R_Q).
\end{equation*}
Suppose that, almost surely, for every round $r < T$, client $k$, and local step $0\leq e\leq E$,
\begin{equation*}
    W_k^{r,e} \in \mathcal N_{\mathbf R_Q}(W^r) \subseteq \mathcal S_{\boldsymbol\gamma,\boldsymbol\delta}^{(Q)}.
\end{equation*}
Suppose also that finite deterministic envelopes $M_{X,q}$ satisfy, almost surely for every round,
\begin{equation*}
    \left\|W_X^{r,(q)}\right\|_2+R_q\leq M_{X,q}, \qquad X\in\{\Delta,B,C,A\}, \qquad q=1,\ldots,Q,
\end{equation*}
where $W_A^{r,(q)}:=A^{r,(q)}$.

Let Assumption~\ref{assum:bounded_layer_inuts} hold uniformly on all these neighborhoods, and let Assumption~\ref{assum:lipschitz_cont_loss} hold uniformly at
the final-layer predictions. The stability margins $\gamma_q,\delta_q>0$, layer-input bounds $L_{x,q}$, loss-regularity constants $L_\ell,H_\ell$, and parameter envelopes $M_{X,q}$ are deterministic and common across rounds, admissible sequence pairs, finite sequence lengths, and token positions.

For each layer, define
\begin{equation*}
    \rho_q:=\exp(-\gamma_q\delta_q)<1.
\end{equation*}

Let $G_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf R_Q}^{(Q)}$ be the common gradient bound defined in Corollary~\eqref{thm:multilayer_fedavg_ssm_convergence}, constructed using the original envelopes $M_{X,q}$.

Assume $0 < \eta\mu \leq 1$, and define
\begin{align*}
    R_{\mu,q} &:= \min\left\{R_q, \frac{G_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf R_Q}^{(Q)}}{\mu}\left[1-(1-\eta\mu)^E\right]\right\}, \qquad q=1,\ldots,Q, \\
    \mathbf R_{Q,\mu} &:= (R_{\mu,1},\ldots,R_{\mu,Q}), \\
    M_{X,q}^{\mu} &:= M_{X,q}-R_q+R_{\mu,q}, \qquad X\in\{\Delta,B,C,A\}.
\end{align*}

Using these reduced parameter envelopes, define
\begin{align*}
    M_{K_\Delta,q}^{\mu} &:= M_{\Delta,q}^{\mu}L_{x,q} + \sqrt D\log2,\\
    M_{K_B,q}^{\mu} &:= M_{B,q}^{\mu}L_{x,q}^2, \\
    M_{K_W,q}^{\mu} &:=ND M_{K_B,q}^{\mu}M_{K_\Delta,q}^{\mu}\left(M_{K_\Delta,q}^{\mu} + \sqrt D M_{A,q}^{\mu}L_{x,q}\right)\frac{\rho_q}{1-\rho_q} \\ 
    &\quad + L_{x,q}^2\sqrt N M_{K_\Delta,q}^{\mu} + L_{x,q}\sqrt D M_{K_B,q}^{\mu},\\
    H_{R_{\mu,q}} &:= \frac{M_{K_B,q}^{\mu}M_{K_\Delta,q}^{\mu}\sqrt{ND}}{1-\rho_q},\\
    J_{R_{\mu,q}} &:= \frac{M_{K_W,q}^{\mu}}{1-\rho_q},\\
    M_{\Lambda_x,q}^{\mu} &:= \rho_q M_{A,q}^{\mu}M_{\Delta,q}^{\mu}H_{R_{\mu,q}} + M_{B,q}^{\mu}L_{x,q}\left(2M_{K_\Delta,q}^{\mu} + L_{x,q}M_{\Delta,q}^{\mu}\right),\\
    M_{\kappa,q}^{\mu} &:= M_{C,q}^{\mu}\left(H_{R_{\mu,q}} + \frac{L_{x,q}M_{\Lambda_x,q}^{\mu}}{1 - \rho_q}\right).
\end{align*}
Initialize $\Theta_0^\mu = \Omega_0^\mu = 0$, and recursively define
\begin{align*}
    \Theta_q^\mu &:= \left[L_{x,q}^2\left(H_{R_{\mu,q}} + M_{C,q}^{\mu}J_{R_{\mu,q}}\right)^2 + (M_{\kappa,q}^{\mu})^2(\Theta_{q-1}^\mu)^2\right]^{1/2}, \\
    \mathcal E_q^\mu &:= \rho_qH_{R_{\mu,q}}
    \Bigg[\left(M_{K_\Delta,q}^{\mu} + M_{A,q}^{\mu}\left(L_{x,q} + M_{\Delta,q}^{\mu}\Theta_{q-1}^{\mu}\right)\right)^2 \\
    &\qquad + 2\left(L_{x,q} + M_{\Delta,q}^{\mu}\Theta_{q-1}^{\mu}\right) + M_{A,q}^{\mu}\left(\frac{1}{4}\left(L_{x,q} + M_{\Delta,q}^{\mu}\Theta_{q-1}^{\mu}\right)^2 + 2\Theta_{q-1}^{\mu}\right)\Bigg] \\
    &\quad + 2\rho_q \left(M_{K_\Delta,q}^{\mu} + M_{A,q}^{\mu}\left(L_{x,q} + M_{\Delta,q}^{\mu}\Theta_{q-1}^{\mu}\right)\right)
    \left(J_{R_{\mu,q}} + \frac{M_{\Lambda_x,q}^{\mu}\Theta_{q-1}^{\mu}}{1 - \rho_q}\right)\\
    &\quad + 2\Theta_{q-1}^{\mu}M_{K_\Delta,q}^{\mu}L_{x,q}\\
    &\quad + 2\left(L_{x,q} + M_{B,q}^{\mu}\Theta_{q-1}^{\mu}\right)\left[L_{x,q}\left(L_{x,q} + M_{\Delta,q}^{\mu}\Theta_{q-1}^{\mu}\right) + M_{K_\Delta,q}^{\mu}\Theta_{q-1}^{\mu}\right] \\
    &\quad + M_{B,q}^{\mu}L_{x,q}\Bigg[L_{x,q}\left(\frac{1}{4}\left(L_{x,q} + M_{\Delta,q}^{\mu}\Theta_{q-1}^{\mu}\right)^2 + 2\Theta_{q-1}^{\mu}\right)\\
    &\qquad\qquad + 2\left(L_{x,q} + M_{\Delta,q}^{\mu}\Theta_{q-1}^{\mu}\right)\Theta_{q-1}^{\mu}\Bigg],\\
    \Omega_q^\mu &:= \frac{M_{C,q}^{\mu}L_{x,q}\mathcal E_q^\mu}{1-\rho_q}\\
    &\quad + 2\left(L_{x,q} + M_{C,q}^{\mu}\Theta_{q-1}^{\mu}\right)\left(J_{R_{\mu,q}} + \frac{M_{\Lambda_x,q}^{\mu}\Theta_{q-1}^{\mu}}{1-\rho_q}\right)\\
    &\quad + 2H_{R_{\mu,q}}\Theta_{q-1}^{\mu} + M_{\kappa,q}^{\mu}\Omega_{q-1}^{\mu}.
\end{align*}

Let
\begin{align*}
    L_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf R_{Q,\mu}}^{(Q)} &:= H_\ell(\Theta_Q^\mu)^2+L_\ell\Omega_Q^\mu,\\
    L_{\mathrm{prox},\mathbf R_{Q,\mu}}^{(Q)} &:= L_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf R_{Q,\mu}}^{(Q)}+\mu,\\
    G_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf R_{Q,\mu}}^{(Q)} &:= L_\ell\Theta_Q^\mu \\
    &= L_\ell\left[\sum_{q=1}^Q L_{x,q}^2\left(H_{R_{\mu,q}} + M_{C,q}^{\mu}J_{R_{\mu,q}}\right)^2\prod_{q'=q+1}^Q(M_{\kappa,q'}^\mu)^2\right]^{1/2},
\end{align*}
where empty products equal one.
These are common deterministic smoothness and gradient
bounds on the reduced neighborhoods
\begin{equation*}
    \mathcal N_{\mathbf R_{Q,\mu}}(W^r) := \prod_{q=1}^Q \mathcal N_{R_{\mu,q}}^{(q)}(W^{r,(q)}).
\end{equation*}

For the client distributions, define
\begin{equation*}
    \bar{\mathcal P}:=\sum_{k=1}^Kp_k\mathcal P_k, \qquad \mathcal H_{\mathrm{TV}} := \sum_{k=1}^Kp_k \operatorname{TV}(\mathcal P_k,\bar{\mathcal P})^2,
\end{equation*}
where $\operatorname{TV}(P,Q):=\sup_{\mathcal A}|P(\mathcal A)-Q(\mathcal A)|$, with the supremum taken over measurable sets $\mathcal A$.

If the step size satisfies
\begin{equation*}
    \eta E L_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf R_{Q,\mu}}^{(Q)} \leq\frac{1}{2}, \qquad
    12\eta^2\left(L_{\mathrm{prox},\mathbf R_{Q,\mu}}^{(Q)}\right)^2E(E-1) \leq 1,
\end{equation*}
then
\begin{align*}
    \frac{1}{T}\sum_{r=0}^{T-1} \mathbb E\|\nabla f(W^r)\|_F^2 \leq& \frac{3(f(W^0)-f^*)}{\eta ET} \\ 
    &+ \frac{3\eta L_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf R_{Q,\mu}}^{(Q)}\left(G_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf R_{Q,\mu}}^{(Q)}\right)^2}{\Xi}\sum_{k=1}^Kp_k^2  \\ 
    &+ \frac{3\eta^2\left(L_{\mathrm{prox},\mathbf R_{Q,\mu}}^{(Q)}\right)^2(E-1)\left(G_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf R_{Q,\mu}}^{(Q)}\right)^2}{\Xi} \\
    &+ 24\eta^2\left(L_{\mathrm{prox},\mathbf R_{Q,\mu}}^{(Q)}\right)^2 E(E-1)\left(G_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf R_{Q,\mu}}^{(Q)}\right)^2\mathcal H_{\mathrm{TV}}.
\end{align*}

Moreover, almost surely, for every round $r < T$, client $k$, layer $q$, and local step $0\leq e\leq E$,
\begin{equation*}
    \left\|W_k^{r,e,(q)}-W^{r,(q)}\right\|_F \leq \min\left\{R_q,\frac{G_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf R_Q}^{(Q)}}{\mu}\left[1-(1-\eta\mu)^e\right]\right\}\leq R_{\mu,q}.
\end{equation*}
Consequently, all local iterates and the aggregated model $W^{r+1}$ lie in
\begin{equation*}
    \mathcal N_{\mathbf R_{Q,\mu}}(W^r) \subseteq \mathcal N_{\mathbf R_Q}(W^r) \subseteq \mathcal S_{\boldsymbol\gamma,\boldsymbol\delta}^{(Q)}.
\end{equation*}
<!-- end corollary-3 -->
<!-- head-14: body_md -->
We apply the federated optimization argument from the proof of Theorem~\ref{cor:fedprox_ssm_convergence} to the full parameter tuple $W=\left(W^{(1)},\ldots,W^{(Q)}\right)$. After vectorization, the Euclidean norm of this tuple is exactly its block Frobenius norm.

First, the pointwise multi-layer gradient bound implies $\left\|g_{k,\Xi}^{r,e}\right\|_F \leq G_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf R_Q}^{(Q)}$, which also bounds each layer block of the mini-batch gradient. Applying the displacement recursion from the single-layer FedProx
proof separately to each layer, and using $0<\eta \mu\leq 1$, gives
\begin{equation}
    \left\|W_k^{r,e,(q)}-W^{r,(q)}\right\|_F \leq \frac{G_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf R_Q}^{(Q)}}{\mu}\left[1-(1-\eta\mu)^e\right].
\end{equation}
Combining this with the assumed containment in the original neighborhood yields the stated bound by $R_{\mu,q}$.

Consequently, all local iterates lie in $\mathcal N_{\mathbf R_{Q,\mu}}(W^r)$. This neighborhood is a product of convex balls, so it also contains their weighted aggregate and the segments used in the smoothness argument.

For every $W\in\mathcal N_{\mathbf R_{Q,\mu}}(W^r)$,
\begin{equation*}
    \left\|W_X^{(q)}\right\|_2 \leq \left\|W_X^{r,(q)}\right\|_2 + R_{\mu,q} \leq M_{X,q}-R_q+R_{\mu,q} = M_{X,q}^{\mu}.
\end{equation*}
The preceding neighborhood estimates therefore apply with the common deterministic envelopes $M_{X,q}^{\mu}$, because their defining expressions are nondecreasing in those envelopes.

Lemma~\ref{lem:multilayer_local_smoothness} supplies the common smoothness constant $L_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf R_{Q,\mu}}^{(Q)}$ for each client objective and for $f$. Adding the proximal penalty makes each local proximal objective $L_{\mathrm{prox},\mathbf R_{Q,\mu}}^{(Q)}$-smooth, where
\begin{equation*}
    L_{\mathrm{prox},\mathbf R_{Q,\mu}}^{(Q)} = L_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf R_{Q,\mu}}^{(Q)} + \mu.
\end{equation*}

Lemma~\ref{lem:multilayer_ssm_variance_bound}, applied conditionally on the complete history before each mini-batch, supplies
\begin{equation*}
    \sigma^2 = \frac{\left(G_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf R_{Q,\mu}}^{(Q)}\right)^2}{\Xi}.
\end{equation*}
The proximal term is deterministic under this conditioning and does not alter the gradient-noise variance. Conditional independence across clients gives the corresponding weighted-noise variance bound $\sigma^2\sum_kp_k^2$.

Lemma~\ref{lem:multilayer_ssm_gamma_tv_bound} supplies the common client-gradient disagreement bound
\begin{equation*}
    \Gamma^2 = 4\left(G_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf R_{Q,\mu}}^{(Q)}\right)^2 \mathcal H_{\mathrm{TV}}.
\end{equation*}
The common proximal term cancels when comparing client gradients with their weighted average at the same parameter tuple.

The remaining local-drift and descent calculations in the proof of Theorem~\ref{cor:fedprox_ssm_convergence} depend only on these smoothness, variance, and disagreement bounds, the conditional sampling assumptions, and convexity of the neighborhoods. Applying those calculations with the multi-layer constants above under the state learning-rate conditions and substituting the defined values of $\sigma^2$ and $\Gamma^2$ yields the desired inequality in Corollary~\ref{thm:multilayer_fedprox_ssm_convergence}, completing the proof. 
\qed
<!-- end head-14 -->
<!-- corollary-4: body_md -->
\label{cor:supp_single_fedprox}
Under the assumptions and step-size conditions of Theorem~\ref{cor:fedprox_ssm_convergence}, given its reduced envelopes $M_X^\mu=M_X-R+R_\mu$, define:
\begin{equation*}
    M_{R_\mu} := (1 + M_B^\mu)(1 + M_C^\mu)(1 + M_\Delta^\mu)(1 + M_\Delta^\mu + M_A^\mu).
\end{equation*}
Then
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:supp_single_fedprox}
    \frac{1}{T}\sum_{r=0}^{T-1} \mathbb E \left\|\nabla f(W^r)\right\|_F^2 = \mathcal O\left(\frac{F_0}{\eta ET} + \frac{\eta M_{R_\mu}^4}{\Xi (1-\rho)^8} + \eta^2 \frac{M_{R_\mu}^2}{(1-\rho)^4}\left(\frac{M_{R_\mu}^2}{(1-\rho)^4}+\mu\right)^2 (E-1) \left(\frac{1}{\Xi}+E\mathcal H_{\mathrm{TV}}\right)
    \right).
\end{equation}
The implicit constant has the same dependencies as in Corollary~\ref{cor:supp_single_fedavg} and is independent of $\mu$.
<!-- end corollary-4 -->
<!-- head-15: body_md -->
The single-layer scaling argument, evaluated with $M_X^\mu$, gives $G_{\mathrm{SSM},R_\mu}=\mathcal O\left(\frac{M_{R_\mu}}{(1-\rho)^2}\right)$ and $L_{\mathrm{SSM},R_\mu}=\mathcal O\left(\frac{M_{R_\mu}^2}{(1-\rho)^4}\right)$.

Consequently $L_{\mathrm{prox},R_\mu} = \mathcal O\left(\frac{M_{R_\mu}^2}{(1-\rho)^4}+\mu\right)$. Substituting these results into Theorem~\ref{cor:fedprox_ssm_convergence} and using the fact that $P_2 \leq 1$ proves the bound.
\qed
<!-- end head-15 -->
<!-- corollary-5: body_md -->
\label{cor:supp_common_gradient}
Under the assumptions of Theorem~\ref{thm:ssm_multi_layer_grad_bound}, the quantities in \eqref{eq:supp_common_layer_constants_start}-\eqref{eq:supp_common_layer_constants_end} satisfy, for every admissible sequence pair $\xi$ of finite length $S$,
\begin{equation*}
    \left\|\nabla_W\mathcal L^{S,(Q)}(W;\xi)\right\|_F \leq L_\ell L_x(H + M_CJ)\sqrt{\sum_{q=0}^{Q-1}\kappa^{2q}}.
\end{equation*}

Furthermore, the following inequalities hold for different values of $\kappa$:
\begin{equation*}
     \left\|\nabla_W\mathcal L^{S,(Q)}(W;\xi)\right\|_F\leq  L_\ell L_x(H + M_CJ) \begin{cases}
     \frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\kappa^2}}, &0\leq\kappa<1,\\
     \sqrt Q, &\kappa=1,\\
     \sqrt{(\kappa^{2Q}-1)/(\kappa^2-1)}, &\kappa>1.
    \end{cases}
\end{equation*}
<!-- end corollary-5 -->
<!-- head-16: body_md -->
All defining expressions~\ref{eq:supp_common_layer_constants_start}-~\ref{eq:supp_common_layer_constants_end} are nondecreasing in the nonnegative parameter, input, and stability upper bounds. As a result, each own-layer factor in Theorem~\ref{thm:ssm_multi_layer_grad_bound} is at most $L_x(H + M_CJ)$, and each
downstream sensitivity is at most $\kappa$. Substitution into the inequality from Theorem~\ref{thm:ssm_multi_layer_grad_bound} gives the right-hand side bounded by $L_\ell L_x(H + M_CJ) \left(\sum_{q=1}^Q\kappa^{2(Q-q)}\right)^{1/2}$. Evaluating the geometric sum proves both claimed inequalities. \qed
<!-- end head-16 -->
<!-- corollary-6: body_md -->
\label{cor:supp_gradient_scaling}
Under the assumptions of Theorem~\ref{thm:ssm_multi_layer_grad_bound}, define
\begin{equation*}
    \mathfrak M_q := \left(1+\|W_B^{(q)}\|_2\right)\left(1+\|W_C^{(q)}\|_2\right)\left(1+\|W_\Delta^{(q)}\|_2\right)\left(1+\|W_\Delta^{(q)}\|_2+\|A^{(q)}\|_2\right).
\end{equation*}
Then, given the $\kappa_q$ defined in that theorem, it holds that:
\begin{equation*}
    \left\|\nabla_W\mathcal L^{S,(Q)}(W;\xi)\right\|_F =\mathcal O\left[ \left(\sum_{q=1}^Q \frac{\mathfrak M_q^2}{(1-\rho_q)^4} \prod_{j=q+1}^Q\kappa_j^2\right)^{1/2}\right].
\end{equation*}
Furthermore, with the common envelopes defined above, let $\mathfrak M := (1+M_B)(1+M_C)(1+M_\Delta)(1+M_\Delta+M_A)$. Then the following holds:
\begin{align*}
    \left\|\nabla_W\mathcal L^{S,(Q)}(W;\xi)\right\|_F &= \mathcal O\left(
    \frac{\mathfrak M}{(1-\rho)^2}\sqrt{\sum_{q=0}^{Q-1}\kappa^{2q}}\right) \\ &= \mathcal O\left(
    \frac{\mathfrak M}{(1-\rho)^2}\begin{cases}
     \frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\kappa^2}}, &0\leq\kappa<1,\\
     \sqrt Q, &\kappa=1,\\
     \sqrt{(\kappa^{2Q}-1)/(\kappa^2-1)}, &\kappa>1.
    \end{cases} \right) .
\end{align*}
The implicit constants depend only on $N,D,L_x,L_\ell$, where $L_ x\geq \max_q L_{x,q}$, and are independent of $Q$, sequence length, parameter norms, and stability factors.
<!-- end corollary-6 -->
<!-- head-17: body_md -->
The single-layer estimates give $L_{x,q}\left(H_q+\left\|W_C^{(q)}\right\|_2J_q\right) \leq C\mathfrak M_q/(1-\rho_q)^2$ for some common constant $C$. Substituting this into Theorem~\ref{thm:ssm_multi_layer_grad_bound} we obtain the first equality of in Corollary~\ref{cor:supp_gradient_scaling}.

Additionally, substituting $L_x(H + M_CJ)=\mathcal O\left(\mathfrak M/(1-\rho)^2\right)$ into Corollary~\ref{cor:supp_common_gradient} proves the second equality. \qed
<!-- end head-17 -->
<!-- corollary-7: body_md -->
\label{cor:supp_common_smoothness}
Under the neighborhood assumptions of Lemma~\ref{lem:multilayer_local_smoothness}, the constants $G_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf R_Q}^{(Q)}$ and $L_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf R_Q}^{(Q)}$ defined in Corollary~\ref{thm:multilayer_fedavg_ssm_convergence} satisfy:
\begin{equation*}
    G_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf R_Q}^{(Q)} \leq \widehat G_Q,
    \qquad L_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf R_Q}^{(Q)} \leq \widehat L_Q.
\end{equation*}
If all common envelopes and regularity constants are fixed independently of $Q$, their depth dependence is
\begin{equation*}
    \left(\widehat G_Q,\widehat L_Q\right)=
    \begin{cases}
      \left(\mathcal O(1),\mathcal O(1)\right), &0\leq\kappa < 1,\\
      \left(\mathcal O(\sqrt Q),\mathcal O(Q^2)\right), &\kappa = 1,\\
      \left(\mathcal O(\kappa^Q),\mathcal O(\kappa^{2Q})\right), &\kappa > 1.
    \end{cases}
\end{equation*}
Here only $Q$ varies: the implicit constants may depend on the fixed common envelopes and on $\kappa$.
<!-- end corollary-7 -->
<!-- head-18: body_md -->
After replacing the layerwise quantities by their common upper bounds, the first-sensitivity recursion yields, for every $q\geq1$,
\begin{equation}
    \Theta_q^2 \leq L_x^2(H + M_CJ)^2\sum_{j=0}^{q-1}\kappa^{2j}.
\end{equation}
Hence,
\begin{equation}
    G_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf R_Q}^{(Q)} =L_\ell\Theta_Q \leq L_\ell L_x(H+M_CJ)\sqrt{\sum_{j=0}^{Q-1}\kappa^{2j}}  =\widehat G_Q.
\end{equation}

For the second-sensitivity recursion, the terms independent of $\Omega_{q-1}$ are bounded by
\begin{equation}
    B_*\left(1+\Theta_{q-1}^2\right),
\end{equation}
This follows because, after replacing the layerwise quantities by their common upper bounds, the terms independent of $\Omega_{q-1}$ form a polynomial of degree at most two in $\Theta_{q-1}$ with nonnegative coefficients. The sum of these coefficients is $B_*$, obtained by setting $\Theta_{q-1}=1$. Since each of $1$, $\Theta_{q-1}$, and $\Theta_{q-1}^2$ is at most $1+\Theta_{q-1}^2$, the displayed bound follows.
Therefore, unrolling the recursion gives
\begin{align}
    \Omega_Q &\leq B_*\sum_{q=1}^Q \kappa^{Q-q}\left(1+\Theta_{q-1}^2\right) \nonumber \\ 
    &\leq B_*\left[\sum_{q=0}^{Q-1}\kappa^q + L_x^2(H + M_CJ)^2 \sum_{q=1}^Q \kappa^{Q-q} \left(\sum_{j=0}^{q-1}\kappa^{2j}\right)\right],
\end{align}
where the first-sensitivity bound was used in the second inequality. Substituting the bounds on $\Theta_Q$ and $\Omega_Q$ into
\begin{equation}
    L_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf R_Q}^{(Q)} = H_\ell\Theta_Q^2+L_\ell\Omega_Q,
\end{equation}
we get:
\begin{equation} 
    L_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf R_Q}^{(Q)} \leq \widehat L_Q.
\end{equation}
Finally, evaluating the geometric sums gives
\begin{equation}
    \left(\widehat G_Q,\widehat L_Q\right) =    
        \begin{cases}   
        \left(\mathcal O(1),\mathcal O(1)\right), &0\leq\kappa<1,\\
        \left(\mathcal O(\sqrt Q),\mathcal O(Q^2)\right), &\kappa=1,\\
        \left(\mathcal O(\kappa^Q),\mathcal O(\kappa^{2Q})\right), &\kappa>1,
        \end{cases}
\end{equation}
which proves the stated depth dependence. \qed
<!-- end head-18 -->
<!-- corollary-8: body_md -->
\label{cor:supp_common_fedavg}
Under the assumptions of Corollary~\ref{thm:multilayer_fedavg_ssm_convergence}, let $\widehat G_Q$ and $\widehat L_Q$ be the common upper bounds in~\eqref{eq:G_Q_hat} and~\eqref{eq:L_Q_hat}, evaluated with the neighborhood-uniform common envelopes introduced above. If
\begin{equation*}
    \eta E\widehat L_Q\leq\frac12, \qquad 12\eta^2\widehat L_Q^2E(E-1)\leq1,
\end{equation*}
then
\begin{equation*}
\begin{aligned}
    \frac{1}{T}\sum_{r=0}^{T-1}\mathbb E\left\|\nabla f(W^r)\right\|_F^2 = \mathcal O\Bigg(&\frac{F_0}{\eta ET} + \frac{\eta\widehat L_Q\widehat G_Q^2P_2}{\Xi}\\
    &+\eta^2\widehat L_Q^2\widehat G_Q^2(E-1)\left[\frac1\Xi+E\mathcal H_{\mathrm{TV}}\right]\Bigg).
\end{aligned}
\end{equation*}
The implicit constant is numerical. For common envelopes and loss-regularity constants fixed independently of $Q$, the depth dependence of $\widehat G_Q$ and $\widehat L_Q$ is explicit in~\eqref{eq:G_Q_hat} and~\eqref{eq:L_Q_hat} and Corollary~\ref{cor:supp_common_smoothness}.
<!-- end corollary-8 -->
<!-- head-19: body_md -->
Corollary~\ref{cor:supp_common_smoothness} gives
\begin{equation*}
    G_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf R_Q}^{(Q)}\leq\widehat G_Q, \qquad L_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf R_Q}^{(Q)}\leq\widehat L_Q.
\end{equation*}
Consequently, the displayed step-size conditions imply those of Corollary~\ref{thm:multilayer_fedavg_ssm_convergence}. Substituting these upper bounds into that corollary and grouping the two local-drift terms proves the stated claim. \qed
<!-- end head-19 -->
<!-- corollary-9: body_md -->
\label{cor:supp_common_fedprox}
Under the assumptions of Corollary~\ref{thm:multilayer_fedprox_ssm_convergence}, retain its original radii $R_q$, effective radii $R_{\mu,q}$, and reduced envelopes $M_{X,q}^{\mu} = M_{X,q}-R_q+R_{\mu,q}$.

For this corollary and its proof, evaluate the existing common bounds $\widehat G_Q$ and $\widehat L_Q$ in~\eqref{eq:G_Q_hat} and~\eqref{eq:L_Q_hat} using
\begin{equation*}
\begin{aligned}
    L_x&:=\max_{1\leq q\leq Q}L_{x,q}, \qquad \rho:=\max_{1\leq q\leq Q}\rho_q,\\ 
    M_X&:=\max_{1\leq q\leq Q}M_{X,q}^{\mu}, \qquad X\in\{\Delta,B,C,A\}.
\end{aligned}
\end{equation*}
All auxiliary quantities in
\eqref{eq:supp_common_layer_constants_start}-\eqref{eq:supp_common_layer_constants_end} and \eqref{eq:supp_common_second_constants_start}-\eqref{eq:supp_common_second_constants_end}, including $\kappa$ and $B_*$, are evaluated with these reduced common envelopes. The loss-regularity constants $L_\ell,H_\ell$ are unchanged.

If $0<\eta\mu\leq1$ and
\begin{equation*}
    \eta E\widehat L_Q\leq\frac{1}{2}, \qquad 12\eta^2\left(\widehat L_Q+\mu\right)^2E(E-1)\leq1,
\end{equation*}
then
\begin{equation*}
\begin{aligned}
    \frac{1}{T}\sum_{r=0}^{T-1} \mathbb E\left\|\nabla f(W^r)\right\|_F^2 = \mathcal O\Bigg(&\frac{F_0}{\eta ET} + \frac{\eta\widehat L_Q\widehat G_Q^2P_2}{\Xi}\\
    &+\eta^2(\widehat L_Q+\mu)^2\widehat G_Q^2(E-1)\left[\frac1\Xi+E\mathcal H_{\mathrm{TV}}\right]\Bigg).
\end{aligned}
\end{equation*}
The implicit constant is numerical and independent of $\mu$.
<!-- end corollary-9 -->
<!-- head-20: body_md -->
