The displacement argument in the proof of Corollary~\ref{thm:multilayer_fedprox_ssm_convergence} places all local iterates in
$\mathcal N_{\mathbf R_{Q,\mu}}(W^r)$. This product of convex balls also contains their weighted aggregate and the segments used in the smoothness argument.

Since $M_{X,q}^{\mu}\leq M_X$ for every layer, the common-envelope construction of Corollary~\ref{cor:supp_common_smoothness} gives
\begin{equation*}
    G_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf R_{Q,\mu}}^{(Q)} \leq\widehat G_Q, \qquad L_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf R_{Q,\mu}}^{(Q)} \leq\widehat L_Q.
\end{equation*}
Therefore,
\begin{equation*}
    L_{\mathrm{prox},\mathbf R_{Q,\mu}}^{(Q)} = L_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf R_{Q,\mu}}^{(Q)}+\mu \leq\widehat L_Q+\mu.
\end{equation*}
The displayed step-size conditions consequently imply those of Corollary~\ref{thm:multilayer_fedprox_ssm_convergence}. Substituting these upper bounds into that corollary and grouping the two local-drift terms proves the stated claim. \qed
<!-- end head-20 -->
<!-- corollary-10: body_md -->
\label{cor:supp_one_local_step}
Under the assumptions of Theorem~\ref{thm:fedavg_ssm_convergence}, let $E=1$. If $\eta L_{\mathrm{SSM},R}\leq1/2$, then
\begin{equation*}
    \frac{1}{T}\sum_{r=0}^{T-1} \mathbb E\left\|\nabla f(W^r)\right\|_F^2 \leq\frac{4F_0}{\eta T} + \frac{4\eta L_{\mathrm{SSM},R} G_{\mathrm{SSM},R}^2P_2}{\Xi}.
\end{equation*}

Similarly, under the assumptions of Corollary~\ref{thm:multilayer_fedavg_ssm_convergence} with $E=1$, if $\eta L_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf R_Q}^{(Q)}\leq1/2$, then
\begin{equation*}
    \frac{1}{T}\sum_{r=0}^{T-1}\mathbb E\left\|\nabla f(W^r)\right\|_F^2 \leq \frac{4 F_0}{\eta T} + \frac{4\eta L_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf R_Q}^{(Q)}\left(G_{\mathrm{SSM},\mathbf R_Q}^{(Q)}\right)^2 P_2}{\Xi}.
\end{equation*}

Both bounds also apply to FedProx with one local step, under the same FedAvg assumptions and step-size conditions,
for every $\mu>0$. No restriction on $\eta\mu$ is needed for this one-step conclusion.
<!-- end corollary-10 -->
<!-- head-21: body_md -->
Setting $E=1$ in Theorem~\ref{thm:fedavg_ssm_convergence} or Corollary~\ref{thm:multilayer_fedavg_ssm_convergence} eliminates both terms containing $E-1$ and makes the second step-size condition automatic. 

For FedProx, initialization at the broadcast model gives
\begin{equation}
    W_k^{r,1} = W^r-\eta\left( g_{k,\Xi}^{r,0}+\mu(W^r-W^r)\right) = W^r-\eta g_{k,\Xi}^{r,0}.
\end{equation}
As such, for every $\mu>0$, its one-step update and weighted aggregate coincide with those of FedAvg. Applying the corresponding FedAvg result proves the claim. \qed
<!-- end head-21 -->
<!-- corollary-11: body_md -->
\label{cor:distributed_learning_rate}
Under the assumptions of Corollary~\ref{cor:supp_single_fedavg}, let $E=1$. Under $\eta L_{\mathrm{SSM},R}\leq1/2$, let $M_B,M_C,M_\Delta,$ and $M_A$ denote uniform upper bounds on $\left\|W_B\right\|_2,\left\|W_C\right\|_2,\left\|W_\Delta\right\|_2,$ and $\left\|A\right\|_2$, respectively, over the $R$-local neighborhoods $\mathcal N_R(W^r)$ and define:
\begin{equation*}
    M_R := (1 + M_B)(1 + M_C)(1 + M_\Delta)(1 + M_\Delta + M_A).
\end{equation*}
Under these conditions, the global optimization trajectory $\{W^r\}_{r=0}^T$ satisfies
\begin{equation*} 
    \frac{1}{T}\sum_{r=0}^{T-1} \mathbb E \left\|\nabla f(W^r)\right\|_F^2 = \mathcal O\left(\frac{F_0}{\eta ET} + \frac{\eta M_R^4}{\Xi(1-\rho)^8}\right).
\end{equation*}
The quantities $N,D,L_x,L_\ell,$ and $H_\ell$ affect only the implicit constant and do not alter the displayed scaling with the optimization parameters, parameter envelopes, stability, or sequence length.
<!-- end corollary-11 -->
<!-- head-22: body_md -->
Setting $E=1$ in Corollary~\ref{cor:supp_single_fedavg} eliminates both terms containing $E-1$, yielding the equality in the Corollary. \qed
<!-- end head-22 -->
<!-- head-23: body_md -->
The results averages show a consistent overall ranking. FedProx achieves the lowest mean perplexity in $34$ of the $36$ metric columns. The two exceptions are last-10 validation perplexity at $\beta=1$, where FedDyn is marginally lower ($40.0$ versus $40.2$), and all-round validation perplexity at $E=64$, where SCAFFOLD is lower ($105.6$ versus $106.6$). On the last-10 metric, FedProx, FedDyn, and SCAFFOLD rank first, second, and third, respectively, at every local-step count, domain setting, and model depth in Tables~\ref{tab:app_fl_by_steps}-\ref{tab:app_fl_by_depth}.
<!-- end head-23 -->
<!-- head-24: body_md -->
With per-client work held fixed, FedAvg's last-10 validation perplexity decreases by approximately $4\%$ from $K=4$ to $K=8$ ($64.2$ to $61.6$), with little further change for the larger client counts ($60.9$ and $61.1$). The training perplexity continues to decrease, from $144$ at $K=4$ to $129$ at $K=32$, potentially reflecting more frequent reuse of smaller local datasets. The three leading methods in the main grid -FedProx, FedDyn, and SCAFFOLD - remain the strongest on last-10 validation perplexity at every $K$. FedAlign and FedSAM remain within $1\%$ of FedAvg, whereas FedAdam has $8$-$9\%$ higher perplexity.
<!-- end head-24 -->
<!-- head-25: body_md -->
Under FedAvg the backbones order as \texttt{hybrid75} ($26.5$) $<$ \texttt{hybrid50} ($29.3$) $<$ \texttt{mamba2} ($30.7$) $<$ \texttt{hybrid50alt} ($31.3$) $<$ \texttt{hybrid25} ($32.7$) $<$ \texttt{attn0} ($42.3$) on the last-10 validation perplexity. Notably, one attention layer in the middle of the stack improves on the pure Mamba2 model by $14\%$. Attention layers placed first (\texttt{hybrid50alt}, \texttt{hybrid50}) or a mostly-attention stack lose that gain, and the attention-only control is $38\%$ worse than pure Mamba2 at this model size and sequence length. The ranking of the algorithms transfers from the pure-SSM backbone to every hybrid, with two exceptions. FedProx is the best method on all six backbones ($-17$ to $-21\%$ relative to FedAvg), and FedAlign and FedSAM stay within $3\%$ of FedAvg everywhere. SCAFFOLD, in contrast, gains $13$--$15\%$ on the hybrids with two or three Mamba2 layers and $10\%$ on \texttt{hybrid25}, but only $4\%$ on \texttt{mamba2} and $1\%$ on \texttt{attn0}, and FedDyn at its fixed $\alpha$ is unstable across architectures ($-2$ to $-6\%$ on \texttt{mamba2}, \texttt{hybrid50}, and \texttt{attn0}, $+6$ to $+11\%$ on the other three hybrids). The server-step methods are the second exception: their penalty grows as Mamba2 layers are replaced, from $+8\%$ (FedAdam) and $+1\%$ (DiLoCo-style) on \texttt{mamba2} to $+40\%$ and $+14\%$ on \texttt{attn0}, whereas FedExP, neutral on \texttt{mamba2}, is $3$-$6\%$ better than FedAvg on every backbone that contains attention.
<!-- end head-25 -->
<!-- head-26: body_md -->
After four epochs, specialists average $89$-$91\%$ accuracy on their own tasks but show little transfer to the other tasks. Task Arithmetic and TIES-Merging outperform weight averaging, with the best evaluated merges reaching $61.3$, $71.8$, and $72.1\%$ for the 130M, 370M, and 780M checkpoints, respectively, compared with $37.4$, $50.4$, and $51.7\%$ for averaging (Table~\ref{tab:app_merge_avg}). Larger checkpoints narrow the gap to specialists, although substantial deficits remain, especially on DBpedia and TREC (Table~\ref{tab:app_merge_tasks}). The sweeps favor Task Arithmetic scales of $\lambda=0.7$-$1$, rather than the averaging scale $\lambda=0.25$, and sparse, rescaled TIES updates, with $(\lambda,d)=(1.3,0.1)$ performing best among the four-epoch settings evaluated (Tables~\ref{tab:app_merge_ta} and~\ref{tab:app_merge_ties}). Additionally, fine-tuning with the proximal penalty $\frac{\mu}{2}\|\theta-\theta^0\|_F^2$ reduces four-epoch averaging accuracy from $37.4\%$ at $\mu=0$ to $26.2\%$ at $\mu=10^{-1}$, near the pretrained baseline (Table~\ref{tab:app_merge_prox}).
<!-- end head-26 -->
